g_{Y}
and a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant connection.

•

L^{2}_{k-\frac{1}{2}}\left(S\right)^{0}_{-\infty}
is the subspace spanned by eigenvectors with non-positive eigenvalues of
{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}
.

Truncating
\mathbf{D}(g_{b})
, we define

\mathrm{ind}_{f}^{\mathrm{APS}}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g_{b}\}}\right)\in KQ\left((B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}\right)

as follows.

Definition 2.35
.

For any
n\geq 0
, we define
\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g_{b}\}}\right)
as the family index with respect to

\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{f}\left(\mathbf{D}(g_{b})\big|_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}}\right),

which is regarded as a family of Fredholm maps between the Hilbert bundles

\mathbf{D}(g_{b})\big|_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}}\colon(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}\times l^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\cong\Gamma(S^{+}_{E})\big|_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}}\longrightarrow\left(\Gamma(S^{-}_{E})\times\left(E\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\Gamma(S)^{0}_{-\infty}\right)\right)\big|_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}}\cong(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}\times l^{2}_{\mathbb{H}},

where
l^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}
is the space of square summable sequences of
\mathbb{H}
with the inner product

\langle\{a_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty},\{b_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\rangle:=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{i}\cdot\overline{b}_{i}.

Applying Kuiper’s theorem
[
Kuiper1965
]
4
4

4

This is a priori about complex vector bundles, but it also works in the real and quaternionic settings, as observed in
[
matumoto1971kuiper
, Section 5]
.
shows that these Hilbert bundles are trivial.

This construction a priori depends on the choices of trivializations, but trivializations are also unique up to homotopy since the infinite-dimensional
\mathbb{H}
-unitary group is contractible. Therefore,
\mathrm{ind}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g_{b}\}}\right)
is independent of the trivializations. However,
\mathrm{ind}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g_{b}\}}\right)
still depends on the choice of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant Riemannian metric on
Y
. In order to eliminate this dependency, we add a shifting term.

Definition 2.36
.

For any
n\geq 0
, we define

\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g_{b}\}}\right):=\mathrm{ind}_{f}^{\mathrm{APS}}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g_{b}\}}\right)-\mathfrak{B}_{n}\left({\bf n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g_{Y})\right)\in KQ\left((B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}\right)\otimes\mathbb{Q}.

The following are the fundamental properties of the invariants:

Proposition 2.37
.

Let us fix a spin homotopy coherent
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action
E
whose boundary family is strict.
The two invariants
H^{+}_{E_{n}}
and
\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n}}\right)
satisfy the following conditions:

(i)

We have the compatibilities

r_{n}\left(H^{+}_{E_{n}}\right)=H^{+}_{E_{n-1}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad r_{n}\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g_{b}\}}\right)\right)=\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n-1},\{g_{b}\}}\right).

(ii)

H^{+}_{E_{n}}
depends only on the isomorphism class of
E
. The invariant
\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n}}\right)
depends on the induced boundary metric of an admissible metric.

(iii)

If we restrict to a point
b\in(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}
, we obtain

(H^{+}_{E_{n}})_{b}\cong H^{+}(X;\mathbb{R})\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n}}\right)\right)_{b}=-\frac{1}{16}\sigma(X)\in KQ(b)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\cong\mathbb{Q}.

Proof.

Proof of (i)
The equality
r_{n}\left(H^{+}_{E_{n}}\right)=H^{+}_{E_{n-1}}
follows from the fact that the choice of sections in (
1
) is unique up to homotopy.
For the second equality, it is sufficient to observe that

r_{n}\left(\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g_{b}\}}\right)\right)=\mathrm{ind}_{f}^{\mathrm{APS}}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n-1},\{g_{b}\}}\right),\qquad r_{n}\left(\mathfrak{B}_{n}\left({\bf n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g_{Y})\right)\right)=\mathfrak{B}_{n-1}\left({\bf n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g_{Y})\right).

These equalities follow directly from the constructions.

Proof of (ii)

For the bundle
H^{+}_{E_{n}}
, it is clear that its isomorphism class is independent of the choice of sections in (
1
).
Let
\{g_{b}\}
and
\{g^{\prime}_{b}\}
be two admissible metrics whose restrictions to the boundary family agree.
The linear homotopy
\{h_{t,b}:=tg_{b}+(1-t)g^{\prime}_{b}\}
gives a 1-parameter family of admissible metrics.
Restricting to
(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}
yields a family of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Fredholm operators

{\bf D}(h_{t,b})|_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}}\colon(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}\times l^{2}_{\mathbb{H}}\cong\Gamma(S^{+}_{E})|_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}}\longrightarrow\left(\Gamma(S^{-}_{E})\times\left(E\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\Gamma(S)^{0}_{-\infty}\right)\right)|_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}}\cong(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}\times l^{2}_{\mathbb{H}},

where we again used Kuiper’s theorem.
By the homotopy invariance of the family index, we have

\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g_{b}\}}\right)=\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g^{\prime}_{b}\}}\right)

as
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant virtual
\mathbb{H}
-bundles over
(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}
.
Since the boundary metrics are the same, it follows that

\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g_{b}\}}\right)=\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n},\{g^{\prime}_{b}\}}\right).

This completes the proof of (ii).

The statement (iii) follows immediately from the definitions.
∎

Fix a compact spin 4-manifold
X
with possibly disconnected boundary
Y
.
Each connected component of
Y=\partial X
is assumed to satisfy
b_{1}=0
.
Under these assumptions, we claim the following:

Theorem 2.38
.

Let

E\colon B\mathbb{Z}_{2}\longrightarrow B\mathrm{Aut}(X,\mathfrak{s})

be a spin homotopy coherent
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action on
X
which is strict on the boundary.
Associated to it, for any
n\geq 0
, there exists a
\mathrm{Pin}(2)
-equivariant fiberwise continuous map, stably written as

\displaystyle{\bf BF}_{E_{n}}\colon\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n}}\right)^{+}\wedge_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}}\mathfrak{B}_{n}\left(SWF(-Y,\mathfrak{t},\tilde{\tau})\right)\longrightarrow(H^{+}_{E_{n}})^{+},

such that
{\bf BF}_{E_{n}}
is a local map of level
b^{+}(X)
.
Here
SWF(-Y,\mathfrak{t},\tilde{\tau})
denotes Montague’s
\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant Seiberg–Witten Floer homotopy type for the restricted equivariant spin structure.
The notations
\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n}}\right)^{+}
and
(H^{+}_{E_{n}})^{+}
refer to the fiberwise one-point compactifications.

Moreover, the diagram

(2)

\displaystyle\begin{CD}\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{m}}\right)^{+}\wedge_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{m}}\mathfrak{B}_{m}\left(SWF(-Y,\mathfrak{t},\tilde{\tau})\right)@>{{\bf BF}_{E_{m}}}>{}>H^{+}_{E_{m}}\\
@A{i_{m}}A{}A@A{i_{m}}A{}A\\
\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{m-1}}\right)^{+}\wedge_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{m-1}}\mathfrak{B}_{m-1}\left(SWF(-Y,\mathfrak{t},\tilde{\tau})\right)@>{{\bf BF}_{E_{m-1}}}>{}>H^{+}_{E_{m-1}}\end{CD}

commutes up to
\mathrm{Pin}(2)
-equivariant stable homotopy for every
m\geq 0
, where
i_{m}
denotes the natural inclusions.

Remark 2.39
.

We call the sequence of maps
\{{\bf BF}_{E_{n}}\}
the
\mathrm{Pin}(2)
-equivariant homotopy coherent Bauer–Furuta invariant of the family
E
.
We expect that
\{{\bf BF}_{E_{n}}\}
is an invariant of the fiber bundle isomorphism class of
E
in a certain category, and that it does not depend on the choice of admissible metrics
\{g_{b}\}
.
However, since our focus in this paper is solely on its existence, we do not address this level of invariance here.

Proof.

First, we have applied the families Bauer–Furuta invariants to this family
E
with up-side-down. An
S^{1}
-equivariant version of this claim has been proven in
[
KT22
, Section 2.3]
under the assumption that
b^{+}(X)=0
and
Y
is connected without assuming
X
is spin. In this proof, we follow the notations given in
[
KT22
]
. However, in the proof, these assumptions are not essentially used, as the existence of a fiberwise map is ensured with
metric-dependent Floer homotopy type
. We see how to replace it with Montague’s spectrum and how the invariants
\mathrm{ind}^{t}(D)
and
H^{+}_{E_{n}}
appear. Also, the compatibility (
2
) was not discussed in
[
KT22
]
, so we also point out how to see it.

Let us denote by

(X,\mathfrak{s})\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow B\mathbb{Z}_{p}

the family of spin
4
-manifolds obtained from a spin homotopy coherent
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action on
X
.
The induced boundary family is written as

(Y,\mathfrak{s}|_{Y})\longrightarrow E_{\partial}\longrightarrow B\mathbb{Z}_{p},

which is isomorphic to the Borel construction of an even
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant spin structure on
Y
.
Take a fiberwise admissible Riemannian metric
g_{b}
on
E
.
Then we have an associated families Seiberg–Witten map with projections: for a real number
\mu
, we have the fiberwise Seiberg–Witten map over a slice

(3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mu}\colon L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda^{1}_{E})_{CC}\oplus L^{2}_{k}(S^{+}_{E})\longrightarrow L^{2}_{k-1}(i\Lambda^{+}_{E})\oplus L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-}_{E})\oplus{\bf V}^{\mu}_{-\infty}(E_{\partial}),

where

•

{\bf V}^{\mu}_{-\infty}(E_{\partial})=V^{\mu}_{-\infty}(E_{\partial})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}E\mathbb{Z}_{2}
,

•

\mathcal{F}^{\mu}
is the fiberwise Seiberg–Witten equation with the fiberwise projection to
{\bf V}^{\mu}_{-\infty}(E_{\partial})
,

•

L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda^{1}_{E})_{CC}
denotes the space of fiberwise
L^{2}_{k}
-valued imaginary
1
-forms on
E
with the fiberwise double Coulomb gauge condition,

•

L^{2}_{k-1}(i\Lambda^{+}_{E})
denotes the space of fiberwise
L^{2}_{k-1}
-valued self-dual
2
-forms on
E
with respect to the fiberwise Riemannian metric
\{g_{b}\}
.

We decompose
\mathcal{F}^{\mu}
as the sum of a fiberwise linear operator
L^{\mu}
and a fiberwise quadratic part
c^{\mu}
.
Moreover, the linear part
L^{\mu}_{b}
is described as the sum of the real operator

L^{\mu}_{b,\mathbb{R}}=\left(d^{+},0,(p^{\mu}_{-\infty})_{\mathbb{R}}\circ r_{b}\right)\colon L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda^{1}_{E_{b}})_{CC}\longrightarrow L^{2}_{k-1}(i\Lambda^{+}_{E_{b}})\oplus V^{\mu}_{-\infty}(\mathbb{R})_{b}

and the quaternionic operator

L^{\mu}_{b,\mathbb{H}}=\left(0,{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{g_{b}},(p^{\mu}_{-\infty})_{\mathbb{H}}\circ r_{b}\right)\colon L^{2}_{k}(S^{+}_{E_{b}})\longrightarrow L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-}_{E_{b}})\oplus V^{\mu}_{-\infty}(\mathbb{H})_{b},

where

•

r_{b}\colon L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda^{1}_{E_{b}})_{CC}\oplus L^{2}_{k}(S^{+}_{E_{b}})\to V(E_{\partial})
is the restriction map on each fiber,

•

(p^{\mu}_{-\infty})_{\mathbb{R}}
and
(p^{\mu}_{-\infty})_{\mathbb{H}}
are projections to the real part
V^{\mu}_{-\infty}(\mathbb{R})_{b}
and quaternionic part
V^{\mu}_{-\infty}(\mathbb{H})_{b}
of
V^{\mu}_{-\infty}(E_{\partial})_{b}
.

We first observe the behavior of the families of operators
L^{\mu}_{b,\mathbb{R}}
: under the assumption that
b_{1}(X)=0
, the operator

L^{0}_{b,\mathbb{R}}\colon L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda^{1}_{E_{b}})_{CC}\longrightarrow L^{2}_{k-1}(i\Lambda^{+}_{E_{b}})\oplus V^{0}_{-\infty}(\mathbb{R})

is injective for any
b\in(B\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{n}
, and hence the fiberwise cokernel gives a bundle over
B\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. From
[
KT22
, Lemma 2.9(ii)]
, this bundle is actually isomorphic to
H^{+}_{E}
. For
L^{\mu}_{b,\mathbb{C}}
with
\mu=0
, the family index of the operator
\{L^{\mu}_{b,\mathbb{R}}\}
is written as the virtual bundle
\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n}})
from its definition. Therefore, we see that the family index
\mathrm{ind}_{f}L^{0}|_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}}
of
L^{0}
parametrized by
(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}
is

(4)

\displaystyle\mathrm{ind}_{f}L^{0}|_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}}\cong\left(-H^{+}_{E_{n}},\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{f}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{g_{b}}\right)\in KO((B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n})\times KQ((B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}).

Now, regarding the compactness and some properties of linear operators, we have the same properties written in
[
KPT24
, Lemmas 4.4–4.7]
, which enable us to take the induced map from finite-dimensional approximations with one-point compactifications of (
3
) as follows: take a sufficiently large subbundle
W_{1}\subset L^{2}_{k-1}(i\Lambda^{+}_{E})\oplus L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-}_{E})
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\left(\mathrm{pr}_{L^{2}_{k-1}(i\Lambda^{+}_{E_{b}})\oplus L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-}_{E_{b}})}\circ L^{0}_{b}\right)+(W_{1})_{b}=L^{2}_{k-1}(i\Lambda^{+}_{E_{b}})\oplus L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-}_{E_{b}})

holds for any
b\in(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}
. We define

W_{0}:=(L^{\mu})^{-1}\left(W_{1}\oplus{\bf V}^{\mu}_{\lambda}\right)\longrightarrow(B\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{n}.

As proven in
[
KPT24
]
,

\displaystyle W_{1}+{\bf V}^{\mu}_{\lambda}+\mathrm{Ker}\,L^{0}-\mathrm{Coker}\,L^{0}\cong W_{0}+{\bf V}^{\mu}_{0}

which is equivalent to

W_{1}+{\bf V}^{\mu}_{\lambda}-W_{0}\cong\mathrm{ind}L^{0}+{\bf V}^{\mu}_{0}

as virtual vector bundles over
(B\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{n}
, where
\mathrm{ind}L^{0}
denotes the family index of
\{L^{0}_{b}\}_{b\in(B\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{n}}
.

Applying the projection, we obtain a family of maps

\text{pr}_{W_{1}\times{\bf V}^{\mu}_{\lambda}}\circ\mathcal{F}^{\mu}|_{W_{0}}\colon W_{0}\longrightarrow W_{1}\times{\bf V}^{\mu}_{\lambda}.

If we denote by
{\bf I}^{\mu}_{\lambda}
the
\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant Conley index of
{\bf V}^{\mu}_{\lambda}
equipped with the
\mathbb{R}
-action from the restricted gradient of CSD, the compactness theorems show that we obtain a
\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant map

BF_{E_{n}}\colon W_{0}^{+}\longrightarrow W_{1}^{+}\wedge{\bf I}^{\mu}_{\lambda},

which can stably be rewritten as

BF_{E_{n}}\colon\mathrm{ind}L^{0}\longrightarrow\Sigma^{-{\bf V}^{0}_{\lambda}}{\bf I}^{\mu}_{\lambda}.

From (
4
), we can again regard it as

BF_{E_{n}}\colon\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{f}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{g_{b}})\longrightarrow H^{+}_{E_{n}}\wedge\Sigma^{-{\bf V}^{0}_{\lambda}}{\bf I}^{\mu}_{\lambda}.

From the definition of the topological part of the family Dirac indices, we see

BF_{E_{n}}\colon\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n}})\right)^{+}\longrightarrow(H^{+}_{E_{n}})^{+}\wedge\Sigma^{-{\bf n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g_{Y})\cdot\mathbb{H}-{\bf V}^{0}_{\lambda}}{\bf I}^{\mu}_{\lambda}.

Now we note that the Conley index
{\bf I}^{\mu}_{\lambda}
can be taken as the Borel construction
I^{\mu}_{\lambda}
with respect to the lift of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action, which ensures

\Sigma^{-{\bf n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g_{Y})\cdot\mathbb{H}-{\bf V}^{0}_{\lambda}}{\bf I}^{\mu}_{\lambda}=\mathfrak{B}_{n}\left(SWF_{\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})\right).

This gives the desired
\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant map

BF_{E_{n}}\colon\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n}})\right)^{+}\longrightarrow(H^{+}_{E_{n}})^{+}\wedge\mathfrak{B}_{n}\left(SWF_{\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})\right).

We also compute

BF^{S^{1}}_{E_{n}}\colon S^{0}\times(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}\longrightarrow(H^{+}_{E_{n}})^{+}\wedge\mathfrak{B}_{n}(S^{0}),

which is homotopic to the induced map from the fiberwise linear injection of codimension
\mathrm{rank}\,H^{+}_{E_{n}}=b^{+}(X)
. Therefore, this map
BF_{E_{n}}
is a local map of level
b^{+}(X)
.

Finally, we consider the compatibility (
2
). Fix an admissible metric
\{g_{b}\}
parameterized by
b\in B\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Then for any
n
-skeleton
(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}\subset B\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, from the above construction, one can construct the Bauer–Furuta invariants

BF_{E_{n}}\colon\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{f}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{E_{n}})\right)^{+}\longrightarrow(H^{+}_{E_{n}})^{+}\wedge\mathfrak{B}_{n}\left(SWF_{\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})\right).

If we consider the corresponding data for
(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n+1}\subset B\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, although we need to choose suitable quantitative constants for
(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n+1}
to see
[
KPT24
, Lemmas 4.4–4.7]
, from the construction we see
BF_{E_{n+1}}|_{(B\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{n}}
is
\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant stably homotopic to
BF_{E_{n}}
. This completes the proof.
∎

The following corollary is now straightforward.

Corollary 2.40
.

We suppose the assumptions of
2.38
. Furthermore, the homotopy coherent group action

E^{\dagger}\colon B\mathbb{Z}_{2}\longrightarrow B\operatorname{Diff^{+}}(-X)

for
-X
induced from
E
admits a spin lift.
Then the element

\mathfrak{B}(Y)\in\mathfrak{F}^{sp,str}_{\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}}

is
\max\{b^{+}(X),b^{-}(X)\}
-stably locally trivial.

Proof.

This follows directly from
2.38
together with the corresponding statement for
-X
.
∎

3.
Equivariant Bauer–Furuta theory for equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
4-manifolds

Although Montague
[
montague2022seiberg
]
developed a general framework for the
\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Floer homotopy type for spin
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-actions, it is also necessary to construct the
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Floer homotopy type in the general case, along with the corresponding
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariants, which recover the theory of Baraglia and Hekmati
[
Baraglia-Hekmati:2024-1
]
. We also establish a gluing theorem that will be used in our construction.

3.1.
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Seiberg–Witten Floer homotopy type

Let
Y
be a rational homology 3-sphere equipped with a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action and a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\mathfrak{s}
. Here we do
not
assume the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action is free.
We fix a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metric
g
.
First, choose a reference
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
connection
A_{0}
such that the associated connection on the determinant line bundle is flat. As shown in
[
Baraglia-Hekmati:2024-1
, Section 3.2]
, for each
\tau\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we can choose a lift
\tilde{\tau}
of
\tau
to the spinor bundle that preserves
B_{0}
. Here we use the assumptions that the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action preserves the isomorphism class of
\mathfrak{s}
and that
b_{1}(Y)=0
.
Let
G_{\mathfrak{s}}
denote the set of unitary automorphisms
u\colon S\to S
of the spinor bundle
S
that preserve
B_{0}
and lift the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
Y
. Then we have a short exact sequence:

1\longrightarrow S^{1}\longrightarrow G_{\mathfrak{s}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}\longrightarrow 1.

This extension is always trivial in our setting, as shown in
[
Baraglia-Hekmati:2024-1
, Section 5]
. Therefore, we may choose a section and identify

\displaystyle G_{\mathfrak{s}}\cong S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

Note that the set of splittings of this extension, i.e., the set of sections

\mathrm{Split}(Y,\mathfrak{s}):=\left\{\text{left inverses }\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to G_{\mathfrak{s}}\text{ of }G_{\mathfrak{s}}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right\},

admits a naturally defined map

\mathcal{S}_{Y,\mathfrak{s}}\colon\mathrm{Split}(Y,\mathfrak{s})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s}),

where
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s})
denotes the set of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures lifting
\mathfrak{s}
. This map is defined as follows: a section
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to G_{\mathfrak{s}}
defines a lift of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on the frame bundle of
Y
to the
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
bundle corresponding to
\mathfrak{s}
, and hence defines a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant lift of
\mathfrak{s}
.

In
4.18
, we will show that
\mathcal{S}_{Y,\mathfrak{s}}
is bijective whenever
p
does not divide
|H_{1}(Y;\mathbb{Z})|
. From now on (in this subsection), we assume that
|H_{1}(Y;\mathbb{Z})|
is not divisible by
p
. Given a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\mathfrak{s}
on
Y
, we denote by
G_{\mathfrak{s}}
the group defined above,
together with the identification
G_{\mathfrak{s}}\cong S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
induced by
\mathfrak{s}
; conversely, if we have chosen a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\mathfrak{s}
, whenever the group
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
appears, it should be understood as
G_{\mathfrak{s}}
.

We fix a flat connection
B_{0}
on
\mathfrak{s}
which is
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant. Now we have an action of
G_{\mathfrak{s}}
on the global Coulomb slice

V=\left(B_{0}+i\ker d^{*}\right)\oplus\Gamma(S)\subset i\Omega^{1}(Y)\oplus\Gamma(S).

Again, by finite-dimensional approximation of the Seiberg–Witten equation, we obtain a
G_{\mathfrak{s}}
-equivariant Conley index
I^{\mu}_{\lambda}(g)
for sufficiently large real numbers
\mu,-\lambda
.
For the details of the construction, see
[
Man03
,
baraglia2024brieskorn
,
montague2022seiberg
]
.
Now a metric-dependent equivariant Floer homotopy type is defined as

SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)=\Sigma^{-V^{0}_{\lambda}(g)}I^{\mu}_{\lambda}(g).

Let
L
be the determinant line bundle of
S
, and fix a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant flat connection
B_{0}
on
L
. We denote by

{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}\colon\Gamma(S)\longrightarrow\Gamma(S)

the
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
-Dirac operator with respect to
B_{0}
.
Notice that
I^{\mu}_{\lambda}(g)
can be taken as a finite
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-CW complex.

Definition 3.1
.

Now, for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we introduce the equivariant correction term

n(Y,\mathfrak{s},\widetilde{\tau},g)_{k}\in\mathbb{C}

defined by

n(Y,\mathfrak{s},\widetilde{\tau},g)_{k}:=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\overline{\eta}^{k}_{{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}}(g)+\sum_{i=1}^{s_{k}}(-1)^{k_{i}+1}\zeta_{p}^{m_{i}}\zeta_{2p}\csc\frac{k_{i}\pi}{p}\cot\frac{k_{i}\pi}{p}\cdot t\left(Y,K_{k,i},g\right),\quad 0<k<p,\\[4.30554pt]
\overline{\eta}_{{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}}^{0}(g)-\frac{1}{8}\eta_{\mathrm{sign}}(g)=n(Y,\mathfrak{s},g),\quad k=0.\end{cases}

where we use the following notations.

•

For the flat connection
A_{0}
and the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metric on
Y
, we associate a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Dirac operator

{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}\colon\Gamma(S)\longrightarrow\Gamma(S).

The equivariant eta-invariant

{\eta}^{k}_{{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}}(g)\in\mathbb{C}

associated to
{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}
is the value at
s=0
of the meromorphic continuation of the equivariant eta function

{\eta}^{k}_{{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}}(g,s)=\sum_{0\neq\lambda\text{ eigenvalue of }{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}}\frac{\mathrm{sign}(\lambda)\cdot\operatorname{Trace}\big((\tilde{\tau}^{k})^{*}\colon V_{\lambda}\longrightarrow V_{\lambda}\big)}{|\lambda|^{s}}\in\mathbb{C},\quad s\in\mathbb{C},

where
V_{\lambda}
denotes the eigenspace of
{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}
for
\lambda
. Note that the finiteness of
{\eta}^{k}_{{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}}(g,0)
is verified in Donnelly
[
donnelly1978eta
]
using an equivariant version of the heat kernel representation of it, together with the small-time asymptotic expansion of the heat kernel, which shows that all potentially divergent terms cancel, leaving a regular value at.
Similarly, for the operator

D_{\mathrm{sign}}:=\begin{pmatrix}*d&-d\\
-d^{*}&0\end{pmatrix}:\Omega^{1}_{Y}\oplus\Omega^{0}_{Y}\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{Y}\oplus\Omega^{0}_{Y},

we have the non-equivariant eta invariant

\eta_{\mathrm{sign}}(g)\in\mathbb{C}.

•

The reduced equivariant eta-invariant

\overline{\eta}^{k}_{{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}}(g)\in\mathbb{C}

is defined as

\overline{\eta}^{k}_{{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}}(g)=\tfrac{1}{2}\left({\eta}^{k}_{{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}}(g)-c^{k}_{{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}}(g)\right),

where
c^{k}_{{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}}(g)
denotes

\operatorname{Trace}\big((\tilde{\tau}^{k})^{*}\colon\ker{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}\longrightarrow\ker{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}\big)\in\mathbb{C}.

•

Let
L
be a connected component of the fixed point set, which is a knot in
Y
. We fix orientations on these components. For the rational Seifert framing of
L
with the fixed
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metric
g
, we obtain a number

t(Y,L,g)\in\mathbb{R}

called
torsion
, defined as follows:
Let
\nabla^{\mathrm{fr}}
be the
\mathrm{SO}(3)
-connection on the
\mathrm{SO}(3)
-frame bundle
\mathrm{Fr}(Y)\to Y
induced by the Levi-Civita connection on
(Y,g)
, and let
\theta=(\theta_{ij})\in\Omega^{1}(Y;\mathfrak{so}(3))
be the connection one-form associated to
\nabla^{\mathrm{fr}}
.
Given a framed, oriented link
L\subset Y
equipped with a framing
\alpha
of
L
, we can trivialize
TY|_{L}
by setting, at each point
x\in L
:

–

e_{1}(x)
is the unit tangent vector to
L
, oriented consistently.

–

e_{2}(x)
is the unit vector pointing in the direction of the framing.

–

e_{3}(x)=e_{1}(x)\times e_{2}(x)
.

This trivialization provides a section
\varphi\colon L\to\mathrm{Fr}(Y)
, and we define the torsion of
L
with respect to
(g,\alpha)
by

t(L,g,\alpha):=-\int_{L}\varphi^{*}\theta_{23}

Note that for any two framings
\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1}
, we have

t(L,g,\alpha_{1})-t(L,g,\alpha_{0})\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}.

If
Y
is a rational homology sphere, then any link
L\subset Y
is rationally null-homologous. We use such a rational canonical framing as
\alpha
. See
[
yoshida1985eta
,
montague2022seiberg
]
for the details.

Also, if we change the orientation of
L
, we have

t(-L,g,\alpha)=-t(L,g,\alpha).

•

We decompose the fixed point set
Y^{\tau^{k}}
of
\tau^{k}
as a union of its connected components
K_{k,1},\cdots,K_{k,s_{k}}
equipped with orientations fixed in the previous item. For each
i=1,\cdots,s_{k}
, we write the action of
\tau^{k}
on the normal bundle of
K_{k,s_{i}}
as
z\mapsto\zeta_{p}^{k_{i}}z
for
k_{i}\in\{1,\cdots,p-1\}
. We also choose
m_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that the the induced action of
\tilde{\tau}^{k}
on
\mathfrak{s}
, considered as a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
Y\times I
, is locally described near any point of
K_{k,s_{i}}\times I
as follows:

[(x,y,z)]\longmapsto\left[\left((-1)^{k_{i}+1}\zeta_{2p}^{k_{i}}x,(-1)^{k_{i}+1}\zeta_{2p}^{-k_{i}}y,\zeta_{p}^{m_{i}}\zeta_{2p}z\right)\right],

where these coordinates describe a principal
U(1)\times U(1)\times U(1)/\{\pm(1,1,1)\}
-bundle, obtained as a reduction of the principal Spin
c
-bundle , covering a principal
SO(2)\times SO(2)
-bundle obtained as the reduction of the framed bundle of
T_{x}(Y\times I)=\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}
for
x\in K_{i,s_{i}}
. Here, we choose an orientation of each component
K_{i,s_{i}}
to have the reduction to a principal
SO(2)\times SO(2)
-bundle. See
Appendix

A
for this description of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Spin
c
structures. Note that this description depends on the choices of orientations of the components.
One can check the term

(-1)^{k_{i}+1}\zeta_{p}^{m_{i}}\zeta_{2p}\csc\frac{k_{i}\pi}{p}\cot\frac{k_{i}\pi}{p}\cdot t\left(Y,K_{k,i},g\right)

does not depend on the choices of orientations.

For a disjoint union
(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
rational homology 3-spheres
\bigsqcup(Y_{i},\mathfrak{s}_{i},\tilde{\tau}_{i},g_{i})
with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metrics, we define

n(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)_{k}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}n(Y_{i},\mathfrak{s}_{i},\tilde{\tau}_{i},g_{i})_{k}\in\mathbb{C}.

Remark 3.2
.

If the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure comes from an even
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Spin structure, our correction term
n(Y,\mathfrak{s},\widetilde{\tau},g)_{k}
coincides with Montague’s
n^{\nu^{k}}
-invariant, which can be seen by removing the term
\zeta_{p}^{m_{i}}\zeta_{2p}
.

Definition 3.3
.

We define

\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\widetilde{\tau},g):=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}n(Y,\mathfrak{s},\widetilde{\tau},g)_{k}\cdot\zeta_{p}^{-kl}\right)\otimes[\mathbb{C}_{[l]}]\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{C}

and call it the
equivariant correction term
.

In order to see several properties of the equivariant correction term, we use the equivariant index of the Dirac operator. Let
(X,\mathfrak{s})
be a compact connected
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
4-manifold bounded by a disjoint union of rational homology 3-spheres equipped with the restricted
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\mathfrak{t}=\mathfrak{s}|_{Y}
. Suppose
X
is equipped with a smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action such that the action preserves the isomorphism class of the
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure.
If we fix an equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
\mathfrak{t}
, we have a unique extension of the equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure to
X
.
We take a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metric on
X
which is product near the boundary and a reference
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
-connection
A_{0}
.
For the action of
\tau^{k}
, suppose the fixed point set is described as the union of 0-dimensional components and 2-dimensional components:

X_{0}^{\tau^{k}}=\{p_{k,1},\ldots,p_{k,m}\},\qquad X_{2}^{\tau^{k}}=\Sigma_{k,1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup\Sigma_{k,n}.

Note that we are not assuming the fixed surfaces
\Sigma_{k,i}
to be closed. Hence the trace index of
{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A_{0}}
for
\gamma
involves the terms
\int_{\Sigma_{k,i}}\tilde{F}_{A^{t}_{0}}
and
\int_{\Sigma_{k,i}}\tilde{F}_{N}
, which depend on the choice of Riemannian metrics, as discussed in
. For simplicity, we rewrite the index formula as

\mathrm{ind}_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{APS}}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A_{0}})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}R_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(S_{k}\left\langle c_{1}(\mathfrak{s}),[\Sigma_{k}]\right\rangle+T_{k}\int_{\Sigma_{k}}\tilde{F}_{N}\right)+\eta_{\gamma}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{Y}),

where
R_{i}
,
S_{k}
, and
T_{k}
are the constants only depending on the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Spin
c
structure restricted to the fixed point locus,
\eta_{\gamma}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{Y})
is the equivariant eta invariant, and
\tilde{F}_{N}
is the curvature of the normal bundle.
Here we are fixing orientations of the components of the fixed point set, but the terms
S_{k}
and
T_{k}
also depend on the choices of orientations so that
\mathrm{ind}_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{APS}}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A_{0}})
is independent of the choices of them.
We will need the following topological lemma.

Proposition 3.4
.

There exists a sufficiently large integer
N>0
such that the disjoint union
\bigsqcup_{N}(Y,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
bounds a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
filling.

Proof.

We follow the proof of
[
montague2022seiberg
, Proposition 2.9]
, which relies on the argument used to prove
[
montague2022seiberg
, Proposition 2.10]
. To adapt the proof to
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures instead of spin structures, it suffices to ensure that the 3-dimensional
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
cobordism group

\Omega_{3}^{\mathrm{Spin}^{c},\mathbb{Z}_{p}}

is an abelian finite group.
Note that there are two components in the equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}
cobordism groups, coming from distinctions of even and odd spin structures, but in our case, there is no such distinction. By the surgery argument given in
[
montague2022seiberg
, Proposition 2.10]
, we can see any
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Spin
c
3-manifold is
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Spin
c
cobordant to a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Spin
c
3-manifold whose
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action is free. This reduces to showing the finiteness of the non-equivariant Spin
c
bordism group evaluated by
B\mathbb{Z}_{p}
:

\Omega_{3}^{\mathrm{Spin}^{c}}(B\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Then, using the Atiyah-Hirzebruch spectral sequence, since the Spin
c
bordism groups
\Omega_{n}^{\mathrm{Spin}^{c}}
are finite for
n=0,1,2,3

[
peterson1968lectures
, Section 8]
, we see
\Omega_{3}^{\mathrm{Spin}^{c}}(B\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is a finite group.
This completes the proof.
∎

Now we record some fundamental properties of
\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\widetilde{\tau},g)
.

Proposition 3.5
.

The equivariant correction term satisfies the following properties.

(i)

\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\widetilde{\tau},g)\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
;

(ii)

Under the augmentation map

\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}\colon R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{Q}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q},

\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\widetilde{\tau},g)
is sent to Manolescu’s original correction term
n(Y,\mathfrak{s},g)
;

(iii)

For a 1-parameter family of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metrics
g_{s}
from
g_{0}
to
g_{1}
, we have

\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\widetilde{\tau},g_{0})-\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\widetilde{\tau},g_{1})=\operatorname{Sf}({\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}(g_{s}))\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

where
\operatorname{Sf}({\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}(g_{s}))
denotes the equivariant spectral flow introduced in
[
lim2024equivariant
]
, see
Appendix

A
for our convention.

Proof.

The proof is essentially similar to that of the fundamental properties of Montague’s correction term given in
[
montague2022seiberg
]
.

Proof of (i)

It is enough to show

\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}n^{k}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\widetilde{\tau},g)\cdot\zeta_{p}^{-kl}\in\mathbb{Q}.

We use
3.4
to take an equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
4-manifold
(X,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X})
bounded by
(Y,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
. Then, from the equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
index theorem (
), we have

\mathrm{ind}_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{APS}}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A_{0}})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}R_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}S_{k}\int_{\Sigma_{k}}\tilde{F}_{A^{t}_{0}}+T_{k}\int_{\Sigma_{k}}\tilde{F}_{N}+\eta_{\gamma}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{Y}),

for the
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
Dirac operator
{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A_{0}}
with respect to a
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
connection
A_{0}
(flat on a neighborhood of the boundary) and a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metric
g
on
X
(product near the boundary), and any
0\neq\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, where
R
,
S
, and
T
are the functions defined in
Appendix

A
.

Now, setting
\gamma=\tau^{k}
with
0<k<p
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{ind}_{\tau^{k}}^{\mathrm{APS}}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A_{0}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{m}R_{k,i}-\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{k,l}\int_{\Sigma_{k}}\tilde{F}_{A^{t}_{0}}+T_{k,l}\int_{\Sigma_{k,l}}\tilde{F}_{N}+\eta_{\gamma}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{Y})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{m}R_{k,i}-\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{k,l}\left\langle c_{1}(\mathfrak{s}),[\Sigma_{k,l}]\right\rangle+T_{k,l}[\Sigma_{k,l}]^{2}+n^{k}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g),

where
\Sigma_{k,i}
is a 2-dimensional connected component of the fixed point set of
\tau^{k}
. Note that we have implicitly used the equality

\int_{\Sigma_{k,i}}\tilde{F}_{N}=[\Sigma_{k,i}]^{2}-\sum_{K_{k,l}\subset\partial\Sigma_{k,i}}\frac{1}{2\pi}\,t(K_{k,l},g),

proved in
[
montague2022seiberg
, Proposition 6.10]
Since the equivariant Dirac index with the APS boundary condition
\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A_{0}})
lies in
R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, we know that

\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathrm{ind}_{\tau^{k}}^{\mathrm{APS}}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A_{0}})\cdot\zeta_{p}^{-kl}\right)\zeta_{p}^{l}\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{p}].

Therefore, it is enough to show

\sum_{i=1}^{m}R_{k,i}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}S_{k,l}\left\langle c_{1}(\mathfrak{s}),[\Sigma_{k,l}]\right\rangle+T_{k,l}[\Sigma_{k,l}]^{2}\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{Q}.

The rationality follows from adapting the argument of
[
montague2022seiberg
, Proposition 6.12]
, together with
3.4
in our setting.

Proof of (ii)

From the definition, we have

n^{0}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)=n(Y,\mathfrak{s},g).

The desired equality follows from the Fourier inversion formula

f(n)=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{m=0}^{p-1}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}f(k)\zeta_{p}^{-mk}\right)\zeta_{p}^{mn},

which holds for any function
f\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\mathbb{C}
.

Proof of (iii)

It is sufficient to show

\displaystyle\begin{cases}t(L,g_{1},\alpha)-t(L,g_{0},\alpha)=-2\pi\int_{L\times[0,1]}\tilde{F}_{N}(g_{s}),\\
n^{k}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g_{0})-n^{k}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g_{1})=\operatorname{SF}^{k}({\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}(g_{s})),\end{cases}

where
\tilde{F}_{N}
denotes the
SO(2)=U(1)
-curvature of the normal directions of
L\times[0,1]
with the restricted Riemann metric, representing the Euler class, and
\operatorname{SF}^{k}({\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{g_{s}})
is the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant trace spectral flow with respect to a family of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metrics
\{g_{s}\}
. The first equality is proven in
[
montague2022seiberg
, Lemma 6.6]
.

To see this relation, we apply (
) to
[0,1]\times Y
equipped with the product
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action and with a family of Riemannian metrics
g_{s}
such that
g|_{\{0\}\times Y}=g_{0}
and
g|_{\{1\}\times Y}=g_{1}
and obtain

\overline{\eta}^{k}(g_{1})-\overline{\eta}^{k}(g_{0})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{k,i}\int_{[0,1]\times K_{k,i}}\tilde{F}_{N}=\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{[k]}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{[0,1]\times Y,\pi^{*}\mathfrak{s},\pi^{*}B_{0}},

where

\pi\colon[0,1]\times Y\to Y
denotes the projection since

[[0,1]\times K_{k,i}]\cdot[[0,1]\times K_{k,i}]=0\text{ and }\langle c_{1}(\mathfrak{s}),[[0,1]\times K_{k,i}]\rangle=0

with respect to the boundary framings.

On the other hand, we have

\operatorname{SF}^{k}({\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}(g_{s}))=\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{[k]}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{[0,1]\times Y,\pi^{*}\mathfrak{s},\pi^{*}B_{0}},

where
\operatorname{SF}^{k}({\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}},\{g_{s}\})
denotes the equivariant spectral flow in the sense of
[
lim2024equivariant
]
, see
Appendix

A
for our convention.

This gives

\overline{\eta}^{k}(g_{1})-\overline{\eta}^{k}(g_{0})=\operatorname{SF}^{k}({\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}(g_{s}))-\sum_{i=1}^{n}T_{k,i}\int_{[0,1]\times K_{k,i}}\tilde{F}_{N}.

This completes the proof of (iii).
∎

Next, we prepare to define an equivariant stable homotopy category that contains our equivariant Seiberg–Witten Floer homotopy types.

Definition 3.6
.

A pointed
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant CW complex
X
is called a
space of type SWF
if
X^{S^{1}}
is
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariantly homotopy equivalent to
\left(\mathbb{R}^{m_{0}}\right)^{+}
.

Now, we define the category
\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
as follows.

Definition 3.7
.

The objects of
\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
are triples
(X,a,b)
, where

(1)

X
is a space of type
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-SWF,

(2)

a\in RO(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,

(3)

b\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
.

Given two objects
(X,a,b)
and
(X^{\prime},a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
, we define the morphism set between them as

\mathrm{Mor}((X,a,b),(X^{\prime},a^{\prime},b^{\prime}))=\left(\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}=a-a^{\prime}\\
\beta-\beta^{\prime}=b-b^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\left[X\wedge\alpha^{+}\wedge\beta^{+},\,X^{\prime}\wedge(\alpha^{\prime})^{+}\wedge(\beta^{\prime})^{+}\right]^{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\right)\big/\sim

where
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
run over finite-rank real
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representations,
\beta,\beta^{\prime}
run over finite-rank complex
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representations, and for two maps

f\colon X\wedge\alpha_{1}^{+}\wedge\beta_{1}^{+}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}\wedge(\alpha^{\prime}_{1})^{+}\wedge(\beta^{\prime}_{1})^{+},\qquad g\colon X\wedge\alpha_{2}^{+}\wedge\beta_{2}^{+}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}\wedge(\alpha^{\prime}_{2})^{+}\wedge(\beta^{\prime}_{2})^{+},

we define
f\sim g
if and only if there exist finite-rank real
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representations
\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{1},\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, finite-rank complex
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representations
\beta^{\prime\prime}_{1},\beta^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, and identifications

\begin{split}\alpha_{1}\oplus\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{1}&\cong\alpha_{2}\oplus\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{2},\\
\alpha^{\prime}_{1}\oplus\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{1}&\cong\alpha^{\prime}_{2}\oplus\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{2},\\
\beta_{1}\oplus\beta^{\prime\prime}_{1}&\cong\beta_{2}\oplus\beta^{\prime\prime}_{2},\\
\beta^{\prime}_{1}\oplus\beta^{\prime\prime}_{1}&\cong\beta^{\prime}_{2}\oplus\beta^{\prime\prime}_{2},\end{split}

such that the maps

f\wedge\mathrm{id}_{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{+}}\wedge\mathrm{id}_{(\beta^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{+}}\quad\text{and}\quad g\wedge\mathrm{id}_{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{2})^{+}}\wedge\mathrm{id}_{(\beta^{\prime\prime}_{2})^{+}}

are
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariantly homotopic.

Similar to the
\mathrm{Pin}(2)
-equivariant case, we have the smash product operation

\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\times\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}},

which allows us to set

SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s})=\bigwedge_{i}SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y_{i},\mathfrak{s}_{i})

for a disjoint union of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant rational homology 3-spheres. As in the
\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant case, the operation
-\wedge-
makes
\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
into a symmetric monoidal category. Furthermore, the process of taking
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant singular cochains also makes sense for objects of
\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
as follows.

Definition 3.8
.

The functor

C^{\ast}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(-;\mathbb{Z}_{p})\colon\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{op}_{C^{\ast}(B(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p});\mathbb{Z}_{p})}

is defined by

C^{\ast}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}((X,a,b);\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\widetilde{C}^{\ast}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X;\mathbb{Z}_{p})[\alpha(a)+2\alpha(b)],

where
\alpha
denotes the augmentation maps on
RO(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
.

Note that there is a forgetting functor

\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}

defined by forgetting the action through
S^{1}\to\mathrm{Pin}(2)
, which is clearly monoidal.

Definition 3.9
.

We define the
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant spectrum class as

SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau}):=\big[(SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g),0,\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g))\big]

as an isomorphism class of objects in the category
\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
.
We also define

\widetilde{H}^{*}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})):=\widetilde{H}^{*+2{\bf n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)\right).

Remark 3.10
.

The pair
(\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
, where
\mathfrak{s}
is a
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
Y
and
\tilde{\tau}
is a lift of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
–action on
Y
to
S
, determines a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
–equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
Y
. Accordingly, we will often write
SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s})
in place of
SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
, with the understanding that
\mathfrak{s}
denotes a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
–equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
Y
.

Since the above definition of equivariant correction term
\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)
is similar to original Montague’s equivariant correction term
[
montague2022seiberg
]
, without any essential change, we see the invariance of choices of Riemann metrics and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant finite dimensional approximations.

Proposition 3.11
.

The spectrum class
SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
is independent of the choices of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metrics.

We also note that there is a duality formula for the equivariant Floer homotopy types. Similar to
[
montague2022seiberg
, Proposition 6.13]
, we have the following duality:

Proposition 3.12
.

Let
(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
rational homology sphere with a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metric
g
, and let
(-Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
denote its orientation reverse. Then

\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)+\mathbf{n}(-Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)=-\ker{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

where
{\partial\mkern-9.5mu/}_{B_{0}}
is the 3-dimensional
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
Dirac operator with respect to a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant flat connection
B_{0}
.

Again, by the same argument given in
[
montague2022seiberg
, Proposition 6.23]
, we see the following.

Proposition 3.13
.

The two spectra
SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
and
SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(-Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
are
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
[S^{0},0,0]
-duals. We denote an
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant duality map by

\eta\colon SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})\wedge SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(-Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})\longrightarrow S^{0}.

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
rational homology 3-sphere that admits a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant positive scalar curvature metric
g
. Then

SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})=[(S^{0},0,\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g))].

If we have an even equivariant spin
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action
(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
, then the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lift on the principal spin bundle can be regarded as a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant lift on the principal
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
bundle. In this case, one can compare the
\mathrm{Pin}(2)
-equivariant and
S^{1}
-equivariant Floer homotopy types.

Lemma 3.15
.

When a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure comes from an even equivariant spin free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action
(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
, our Floer homotopy type
SWF(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
can be recovered from Montague’s homotopy type through the forgetting map

\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}.

We observe that, by following the construction of
\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, we can define the space-level
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant local equivalence group as follows.

Definition 3.16
.

Given two objects
(X,a,b)
and
(X^{\prime},a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
of
\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, a morphism
f
between them, represented as an
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant map

f\colon X\wedge\alpha^{+}\wedge\beta^{+}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}\wedge(\alpha^{\prime})^{+}\wedge(\beta^{\prime})^{+},

is a
local map
if
f^{S^{1}}
is (non-equivariantly) a homotopy equivalence. We say that
(X,a,b)
and
(X^{\prime},a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
are
locally equivalent
if there exist local maps between them in both directions.

Definition 3.17
.

We define

\mathfrak{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}=\frac{\left\{\text{isomorphism classes of objects of }\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\right\}}{\text{local equivalence}},

where the group operation is given by
-\wedge-
. As in the
\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant case,
\mathfrak{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is a well-defined abelian group.

3.1.1.
Recovering Baraglia–Hekmati’s theory

In this subsection, we will prove that the spectrum
SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})
recovers the invariants of Baraglia–Hekmati
[
Baraglia-Hekmati:2024-1
]
.

Let
Y
be a rational homology 3-sphere equipped with a free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action
\tau\colon Y\to Y
and a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
. Then we have the spectrum
SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})\in\mathfrak{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
.

Lemma 3.18
.

If we take cohomology, we recover Baraglia–Hekmati’s
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Floer cohomology:

\widetilde{H}^{*+2{n}_{\mathbb{C}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)\right)\cong\widetilde{H}_{*}\left(C^{*}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s}))\right)

as modules over the ring
H^{*}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}:=H^{*}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\ast;\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, where
SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)

is the metric-dependent Seiberg–Witten Floer homotopy type introduced in
[
Baraglia-Hekmati:2024-1
]
.

Note that

H^{*}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{Z}_{2}[U,\theta]&\text{if $p=2$,}\\
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[U,R,S]/(R^{2})&\text{if $p>2$,}\end{cases}

where
\deg(U)=\deg(S)=2
and
\deg(\theta)=\deg(R)=1
and
SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}({Y},\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)
denotes the metric-dependent Floer homotopy type.

Proof.

We use the fact that

\alpha\left({\bf n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau},g)\right)=n(Y,\mathfrak{s},g)

from
3.5
(ii), together with the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Thom isomorphism theorem with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-coefficients.
∎

Remark 3.19
.

The isomorphism class of the module

\widetilde{H}^{*}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(SWF(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})\right)

depends on the choice of equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure, as pointed out in
[
baraglia2024brieskorn
]
.

Let us also review the equivariant Frøyshov invariants, developed by Baraglia and Hekmati in
[
baraglia2024brieskorn
]
.
Let
(X,a,b)
be an element of
\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
.
The inclusion of the fixed points
\iota\colon X^{S^{1}}\to X
induces a map

\iota^{*}\colon U^{-1}H^{*}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(X;\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)\longrightarrow U^{-1}\widetilde{H}^{*}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(X^{S^{1}}\right)\cong U^{-1}H^{*}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}.

We now recall the sequence of invariants
\{\delta_{G,j}(X,a,b)\}
:

(i)

If
p=2
, we define

\delta_{G,j}(X,a,b):=\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\min\left\{i\;\middle|\;\exists\,x\in\widetilde{H}^{\,i-a-2b}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(X;\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right),\ \iota^{*}x\equiv U^{k}\theta^{j}\bmod{\theta^{j+1}}\text{ for some }k\geq 0\right\}-j\right).

(ii)

If
p>2
, we define

\delta_{G,j}(X,a,b):=\min\left\{i\;\middle|\;\exists\,x\in\widetilde{H}^{\,i-a-2b}_{G}\left(X;\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right),\ \iota^{*}x\equiv S^{j}U^{k}\bmod{\left(S^{j+1},\;RS^{j+1}\right)}\text{ for some }k\geq 0\right\}-2j.

We then set

\delta_{G,j}\left(Y,\mathfrak{s}\right):=\delta_{G,j}\left(SWF(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})\right)\in\mathbb{Q}.

By
3.18
, this agrees with the equivariant Frøyshov invariants originally introduced in
[
Baraglia-Hekmati:2024-1
]
.

Remark 3.20
.

Let
K
be a knot in
S^{3}
. For each prime
p
, by
3.9
we obtain a metric-independent

S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
–equivariant Seiberg–Witten Floer homotopy type of the
p
–fold branched cover
\Sigma_{p}(K)
with the unique spin structure
\mathfrak{s}_{0}
, equipped with a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
–lift:

SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Sigma_{p}(K),\mathfrak{s}_{0}).

For any
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we may then consider the
[k]
–fixed point spectrum

(5)

\displaystyle SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Sigma_{p}(K),\mathfrak{s}_{0})^{[k]},

which defines a knot invariant. At first sight, (
5
) may appear to depend on the choice of equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures. However, it can be canonically regarded as a knot invariant as follows.

•

When
p=2
, there are precisely two equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures
{\tau}_{1}
and
{\tau}_{2}
on
\mathfrak{s}_{0}
covering the deck transformation
\tau:\Sigma_{2}(K)\to\Sigma_{2}(K)
. These are given by

\tau_{1}=i\,\widetilde{\tau},\qquad\tau_{2}=-i\,\widetilde{\tau},

where
\widetilde{\tau}
is an order-four lift of
\tau
to
\mathfrak{s}_{0}
commuting with the principal
\mathrm{Spin}(4)
–action, as observed in
[
iida2024monopoles
]
. One then has
j\tau_{1}=\tau_{2}j
on the configuration space of
\Sigma_{2}(K)
. Consequently,

SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(\Sigma_{2}(K),\mathfrak{s}_{0})^{\tau_{1}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(\Sigma_{2}(K),\mathfrak{s}_{0})^{\tau_{2}}

are homeomorphic as
S^{1}
–spaces, where the
S^{1}
–action is given by complex conjugation
z\mapsto\overline{z}
.

•

When
p
is odd, it was shown in
[
montague2022seiberg
, Proposition 2.2]
that the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
–lift
\widetilde{\tau}
of the deck transformation to the principal
\mathrm{Spin}(4)
–bundle is uniquely determined (whereas
-\widetilde{\tau}
gives an odd lift). Passing through the natural map
\mathrm{Spin}(4)\to\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(4)
then yields a canonical
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
–equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
\mathfrak{s}_{0}
.

Since this construction is compatible with orbifold gauge theory, we define the
orbifold Seiberg–Witten Floer homotopy type
of
K
by

SWF^{(p),[k]}_{\mathrm{ofd}}(K):=SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Sigma_{p}(K),\mathfrak{s}_{0})^{[k]},\qquad[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p},

as an
S^{1}
–equivariant stable pointed homotopy type.
When
p=2
, this invariant is expected to be related to Jiakai Li’s monopole Floer homology of webs
[
li2023monopole
]
in the case with no real locus, which may be viewed as a version of monopole Floer homology for
3
–orbifolds with cone angle
\pi
obtained from knots.
From
[
iida2024monopoles
, Theorem 1.16]
, we see that
SWF^{(2),[1]}_{\mathrm{ofd}}(K)
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
–homology sphere for any
K\subset S^{3}
.

3.2.
Equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariants

In this section, we shall discuss several properties of
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariants, which will be used to construct an equivariant map from the lattice homotopy type to the equivariant Seiberg–Witten Floer homotopy type.

Let
(X,\mathfrak{s})
be a compact connected
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
4-manifold bounded by a rational homology 3-sphere equipped with the restricted
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\mathfrak{t}=\mathfrak{s}|_{Y}
. Suppose
X
is equipped with a smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action such that the action preserves the isomorphism class of the
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure, the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action is free on
Y
, and
b_{1}(X)=0
.
If we fix an equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
\mathfrak{t}
, we obtain a unique extension of the equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
X
.
We take a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metric on
X
which is product near the boundary and a reference
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
connection
A_{0}
.
For the action of
\tau^{k}
, suppose the fixed point set is described as the union of 0-dimensional components and 2-dimensional components:

X_{0}^{\tau^{k}}=\{p_{k,1},\ldots,p_{k,m}\},\qquad X^{\tau^{k}}_{2}=\Sigma_{k,1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup\Sigma_{k,n}.

In order to state the results, we introduce two topological invariants:

•

The first invariant is

H^{+}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X):=(\mathbb{Z}_{p}\longrightarrow O(H^{+}(X;\mathbb{R})))\in RO(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

•

The second invariant is

\displaystyle\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{k}(D,\tau)\cdot\zeta_{p}^{-kl}\right)\zeta_{p}^{l}\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{C},

where

\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\tau^{k}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}:=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}R_{k,i}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(S_{k,i}\langle c_{1}(L),[\Sigma_{k,i}]\rangle+T_{k,i}[\Sigma_{k,i}]^{2}\right)&\text{if }k\neq 0,\\[6.45831pt]
\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\left(c_{1}(\mathfrak{s})^{2}-\sigma(X)\right)&\text{if }k=0,\end{cases}

where the data
R,S,T
are determined by the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on the fixed point locus (see
Appendix

A
for details).

These invariants
H^{+}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\in RO(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{C}
depend only on the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant structure and the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure.
Moreover, we have:

Lemma 3.21
.

The quantity
\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}
satisfies the following properties:

(i)

\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
,

(ii)

\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}\right)=\tfrac{1}{8}\left(c_{1}(\mathfrak{s})^{2}-\sigma(X)\right)
, where
\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}
denotes the complex augmentation map,

(iii)

When
X
is a closed 4-manifold,
\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and coincides with the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Dirac index.

Proof.

(i) follows from the capping-off argument
[
montague2022seiberg
, Proposition 6.12]
, combined with
3.4
.
(ii) follows from the Fourier inversion formula, as in the proof of part (ii) of
3.5
.
(iii) follows from
applied to a closed
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
4-manifold.
∎

Now we state our result on the equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariants for equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures:

Proposition 3.22
.

With these data, we associate an
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant map

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X,\mathfrak{s})\colon\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}\right)^{+}\longrightarrow H^{+}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)^{+}\wedge SWF(Y,\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})

such that
BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{S^{1}}
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-homotopy equivalence, giving a well-defined morphism in the category
\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
.
Moreover, if we forget the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action,
BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X,\mathfrak{s})
recovers the ordinary
S^{1}
-equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariant
BF_{S^{1}}(X,\mathfrak{s})
defined in
[
Man03
,
Kha15
]
.

A
\mathrm{Spin}
version of this map is constructed in
[
montague2022seiberg
, Section 7.2]
.

Proof.

When defining the Bauer–Furuta invariants, a technical point is the properness of the monopole map with boundary conditions. Since this is not the main issue here, we omit the details. The main part concerns how the representations
H^{+}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
and
\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}
appear in the setting of relative Bauer–Furuta invariants. Let us briefly describe their appearance.

Let
S=S^{+}\oplus S^{-}
be the spinor bundle of
\mathfrak{s}
equipped with a lift
\tilde{\tau}
of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action. Take a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metric
g
and a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
connection
A_{0}
which is flat near the boundary. Consider the Seiberg–Witten equation combined with the projection

\mathcal{F}+\text{pr}^{\nu}_{-\infty}\colon\bigl(A_{0}+(i\Omega_{X}^{1})_{CC}\bigr)\times\Gamma(S^{+})\longrightarrow i\Omega_{X}^{+}\times\Gamma(S^{-})\times V(Y)^{\nu}_{-\infty},

which is
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant. Here,
\mathcal{F}
is the Seiberg–Witten equation on
X
,
\text{pr}^{\nu}_{-\infty}
is the projection to
V(Y)^{\nu}_{-\infty}
,
(i\Omega_{X}^{1})_{CC}
is the space of
i
-valued 1-forms with double Coulomb gauge condition as in
[
Kha15
]
, and
i\Omega_{X}^{+}
is the space of
i
-valued self-dual forms. All functional spaces are completed with suitable Sobolev norms. We decompose
\mathcal{F}
as the sum
L+C
, where
L=({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A_{0}}^{+},d^{+})
. Pick a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant finite-dimensional subspace
U^{\prime}\subset i\Omega_{X}^{+}\times\Gamma(S^{-})
and an eigenvalue
\lambda\ll 0
such that

U^{\prime}\oplus V^{\nu}_{\lambda}\subset i\Omega_{X}^{+}\times\Gamma(S^{-})\times V^{\nu}_{-\infty}

contains
\operatorname{Coker}(L\oplus\text{pr}^{\nu}_{-\infty})
.

Next, let

U:=(L\oplus(\Pi^{\nu}\circ r))^{-1}(U^{\prime}\oplus V^{\nu}_{\lambda})\subset U_{W},

and consider the projected map

\pi_{U^{\prime}\oplus V^{\nu}_{\lambda}}\circ\mathcal{F}|_{U}\colon U\longrightarrow U^{\prime}\oplus V^{\nu}_{\lambda}

between finite-dimensional subspaces. If
U^{\prime}
and
-\lambda
are chosen large enough, this induces a based map

\psi_{U^{\prime},\nu,\lambda}\colon U^{+}\longrightarrow(U^{\prime})^{+}\wedge I^{\nu}_{\lambda}

from the one-point compactification of
U
to a suspension of the
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Conley index
I^{\nu}_{\lambda}
.

We define the map

\psi_{U^{\prime},\nu,\lambda}\colon(r\mathbb{R}+h\mathbb{C})^{+}\longrightarrow(r^{\prime}\mathbb{R}+h^{\prime}\mathbb{C})^{+}\wedge I^{\nu}_{\lambda},

where

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} r-r^{\prime}&=V^{0}_{\lambda}(\mathbb{R})-H^{+}(W,\tau)\in RO(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),\\
h-h^{\prime}&=V^{0}_{\lambda}(\mathbb{C})+\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{X,\mathfrak{s},A_{0},g}\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p}),\end{aligned}\end{cases}

and
\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{X,\mathfrak{s},A_{0},g}
denotes the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant APS index of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Dirac operator on
W
.

Using
, we obtain

h-h^{\prime}=V^{0}_{\lambda}(\mathbb{C})+\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},\sigma,g)-\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

This ensures the existence of the map. The well-definedness is routine, so we omit it. Moreover, since
\alpha(\mathbf{n}(Y,\mathfrak{s},g,\tau))=n(Y,\mathfrak{s},g)
and
\alpha(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/})=\mathrm{ind}^{t}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}
, this construction obviously recovers the usual
S^{1}
-equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariants when we forget the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action. This completes the proof.
∎

Similarly, by combining the duality maps stated in
3.13
, one can also treat a
4
-manifold
X
with several boundary components. We state the result without proof:

Proposition 3.23
.

Let
X
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
cobordism from
\bigsqcup_{1\leq i\leq n}Y_{i}
to
\bigsqcup_{1\leq i\leq m}Y^{\prime}_{i}
satisfying the following conditions:

•

b_{1}(X)=0
,

•

b_{1}(Y_{i})=b_{1}(Y_{i}^{\prime})=0
,

•

the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action preserves each component
Y_{i}
and
Y_{i}^{\prime}
.

Associated with this, one has an
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant map

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X,\mathfrak{s})\colon\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}^{+}\wedge\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq n}SWF(Y_{i},\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})\longrightarrow H^{+}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)^{+}\wedge\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq m}SWF(Y^{\prime}_{i},\mathfrak{s},\tilde{\tau})

such that
BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{S^{1}}
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-homotopy equivalence, regarded as a well-defined morphism in the category
\mathcal{C}^{sp}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
.

Remark 3.24
.

We record a remark related to orbifold Seiberg–Witten theory. As in
3.20
, for a knot
K\subset S^{3}
, a prime
p
, and
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we obtain an orbifold Seiberg–Witten Floer homotopy type

SWF^{(p),[k]}_{\mathrm{ofd}}(K)

realized as a fixed-point spectrum.

Suppose we have a properly embedded surface
S
in
[0,1]\times S^{3}\#X
from
K
to
K^{\prime}
, where
X
is a fixed oriented closed
4
–manifold, such that the homology class

[S]\in H_{2}([0,1]\times S^{3}\#X,\partial;\mathbb{Z})

is divisible by
p
, and that there is an invariant Spin
c
structure
\mathfrak{s}
on the
p
–fold cover
\Sigma_{p}(S)
. Then, from
3.23
, we obtain an
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
–equivariant map

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Sigma_{p}(S),\mathfrak{s})\colon\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}^{+}\wedge SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Sigma_{p}(K),\mathfrak{s}_{0})\longrightarrow H^{+}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Sigma_{p}(S))^{+}\wedge SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Sigma_{p}(K^{\prime}),\mathfrak{s}_{0}^{\prime}).

For any
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we may take the fixed-point part:

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Sigma_{p}(S),\mathfrak{s})^{[k]}\colon(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}^{+})^{[k]}\wedge SWF^{(p),[k]}_{\mathrm{ofd}}(K)\longrightarrow H^{+}(X)^{+}\wedge SWF^{(p),[k]}_{\mathrm{ofd}}(K^{\prime}),

which we call the
orbifold Bauer–Furuta invariant
for
S
, denoted
BF^{(p),[k]}_{\mathrm{ofd}}(S,\mathfrak{s})
.

If we restrict attention only to the fixed-point part of the theory, the divisibility condition on
[S]
is not required. Indeed, for a surface
S\subset[0,1]\times S^{3}\#X
, one obtains a corresponding
4
–orbifold with boundary and cone angle
2\pi/p
for any prime
p
. For any orbifold Spin
c
structure
\mathfrak{s}
on this orbifold, we then obtain the corresponding
S^{1}
–equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariant
BF^{(p),[k]}_{\mathrm{ofd}}(S,\mathfrak{s})
. For further discussion of orbifold Spin
c
structures and orbifold Seiberg–Witten theory with codimension-two singularities, see
[
baldridge2001seiberg
,
chen2004pseudoholomorphic
,
chen2006smooth
,
chen2012seiberg
,
lebrun2015edges
,
chen2020class
]
.

Our goal for the rest of this section is to establish the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant adjunction relation stated in
3.31
.
We first state a general theorem.

Suppose
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
4
-manifolds
X_{1},X_{2}
, and
X_{3}
, possibly with several boundary components, admit a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
decomposition

X_{i}=X_{i,1}\cup_{Y^{\prime}}X_{i,2},

cut along a rational homology
3
-sphere
Y^{\prime}
equipped with a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant positive scalar curvature metric.
We assume that

\partial X_{i,j}\smallsetminus Y^{\prime}

is a disjoint union of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
rational homology
3
-spheres.
Define

W_{1}:=X_{1,1}\cup_{Y^{\prime}}X_{2,2},\qquad W_{2}:=X_{2,1}\cup_{Y^{\prime}}X_{3,2},\qquad W_{3}:=X_{3,1}\cup_{Y^{\prime}}X_{1,2}.

Suppose
b_{1}(X_{i,j})=b_{1}(W_{i,j})=0
.

In this situation, by following the strategy of
[
bauer2004stable
]
, we obtain the following result:

Proposition 3.25
.

We have the equality

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{1})\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{2})\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{3})=BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{1})\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{2})\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{3})

up to
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant stable homotopy, where
BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{i})
and
BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{i})
denote the
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariants of the forms

\displaystyle BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{i})

\displaystyle\colon(\mathrm{ind}^{t}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{X_{i}})^{+}\longrightarrow(H^{+}(X_{i}))^{+}\wedge SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X_{i}),

\displaystyle BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{i})

\displaystyle\colon(\mathrm{ind}^{t}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{i}})^{+}\longrightarrow(H^{+}(W_{i}))^{+}\wedge SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial W_{i}).

Remark 3.26
.

We expect that a general gluing formula should hold in a general situation without assuming
Y^{\prime}
admits a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant positive scalar curvature metric, following the techniques of Manolescu
[
manolescu2007gluing
]
and Khandhawit–Lin–Sasahira
[
khandhawit2023unfolded
]
. For our application, however,
3.31
is sufficient. We will prove this proposition by using a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant version of Bauer’s gluing technique
[
bauer2004stable
]
, which yields a shorter proof than that in
[
manolescu2007gluing
,
khandhawit2023unfolded
]
. Note that this wedge sum formula is proven in
[
bauer2004stable
]
in the case
Y^{\prime}=S^{3}
without
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action. The key ingredient of Bauer’s argument is the existence of a positive scalar curvature metric on
S^{3}
. In our setting, we replace it with a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant positive scalar curvature metric on
Y^{\prime}
.

Proof of
3.25
.

We give a sketch of the proof following
[
bauer2004stable
]
. The presence of the double Coulomb gauge conditions, which are not considered in
[
bauer2004stable
]
, requires one additional homotopy, as in
[
khandhawit2023unfolded
]
.

Step 1
:

We consider families of Riemannian 4-manifolds
g_{L}
and
g^{\prime}_{L}
on

\displaystyle X(L)

\displaystyle=X_{1,1}\cup[-L,L]\times Y^{\prime}\cup X_{1,2}\sqcup X_{2,1}\cup[-L,L]\times Y^{\prime}\cup X_{2,2}\sqcup X_{3,1}\cup[-L,L]\times Y^{\prime}\cup X_{3,2},

\displaystyle W(L)

\displaystyle=X_{1,1}\cup[-L,L]\times Y^{\prime}\cup X_{2,2}\sqcup X_{2,1}\cup[-L,L]\times Y^{\prime}\cup X_{3,2}\sqcup X_{3,1}\cup[-L,L]\times Y^{\prime}\cup X_{1,2},

satisfying the following conditions:

•

g_{L}
and
g^{\prime}_{L}
restrict to
dt^{2}+g_{Y^{\prime}}
on the components
[-L,L]\times Y^{\prime}
,

•

g_{L}
and
g^{\prime}_{L}
are product metrics near the boundaries of
X(L)
and
W(L)
,

•

g_{L}|_{X_{i,j}}
and
g^{\prime}_{L}|_{X_{i,j}}
are independent of
L
.

Take
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
connections
A_{X}
and
A_{W}
on
X(L)
and
W(L)
that are flat near the boundaries and on each
[-L,L]\times Y^{\prime}
.
We further require
A_{X}|_{[-L,L]\times Y^{\prime}}=A_{W}|_{[-L,L]\times Y^{\prime}}
as connections.

Step 2
:

We first move the global slice condition to the Seiberg–Witten map.
Consider the Seiberg–Witten equations on
X(L)
and
W(L)
:

(6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{X(L)}+\text{pr}

\displaystyle\colon(A_{X}+L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda_{X(L)}^{1})_{CC})\times L^{2}_{k}(S^{+})\longrightarrow iL^{2}_{k-1}(\Lambda_{X(L)}^{+})\times L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-})\times V(\partial X(L))^{\mu}_{-\infty},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{W(L)}+\text{pr}

\displaystyle\colon(A_{W}+L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda_{W(L)}^{1})_{CC})\times L^{2}_{k}(S^{+})\longrightarrow iL^{2}_{k-1}(\Lambda_{W(L)}^{+})\times L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-})\times V(\partial W(L))^{\mu}_{-\infty}.

where
\mathcal{F}+\text{pr}
denotes the Seiberg–Witten map defined by

(A,\Phi)\longmapsto\left(\rho(F^{+}(A))-(\Phi,\Phi)_{0},\ {\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A}(\Phi),\ p^{\mu}_{-\infty}\circ r(A,\Phi)\right).

Here
\rho
is the Clifford multiplication,
F^{+}(A)
is the curvature of the
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
connection,
(\Phi,\Phi)_{0}
is the traceless part of
\Phi\otimes\Phi^{*}
,
{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A}
is the
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
Dirac operator, and

r\colon(A_{X}+L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda_{X(L)}^{1})_{CC})\times L^{2}_{k}(S^{+})\longrightarrow V(\partial X(L))

is the restriction map to the global slice
V(\partial X(L))
of the configuration space of
\partial X(L)
.
The space
L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda_{X(L)}^{1})_{CC}
denotes the double Coulomb sliced 1-forms, and we use the same notation for
W(L)
.
Note that
\partial X(L)
and
\partial W(L)
are disjoint unions of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
rational homology 3-spheres, independent of
L
.
If we write
\partial X(L)=\sqcup_{i}Y_{i}
, we set

V(\partial X(L)):=V(Y_{1})\times\cdots\times V(Y_{n}),

where each
V(Y_{i})
is the usual global slice
i\ker d^{*}\times\Gamma(S_{Y_{i}})
.
We use the
L^{2}_{k-\frac{1}{2}}
-completion for
V(\partial X(L))^{\mu}_{-\infty}
.
These maps are
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant.

By the argument in
[
khandhawit2023unfolded
]
, in this step we claim that these maps are
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariantly c-stably homotopic
to

(7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{X(L)}+d^{*}+\text{pr}

\displaystyle\colon(A_{X}+L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda_{X(L)}^{1})_{C})\times L^{2}_{k}(S^{+})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow iL^{2}_{k-1}(\Lambda_{X(L)}^{0}\oplus\Lambda_{X(L)}^{+})\times L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-})\times V(\partial X(L))^{\mu}_{-\infty},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{W(L)}+d^{*}+\text{pr}

\displaystyle\colon(A_{W}+L^{2}_{k}(i\Omega_{W(L)}^{1})_{C})\times L^{2}_{k}(S^{+})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow iL^{2}_{k-1}(\Lambda_{W(L)}^{0}\oplus\Lambda_{W(L)}^{+})\times L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-})\times V(\partial W(L))^{\mu}_{-\infty},

which are defined by

(A,\Phi)\longmapsto\bigl(d^{*}(A-A_{0}),\ \rho(F^{+}(A))-(\Phi,\Phi)_{0},\ {\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A}(\Phi),\ p^{\mu}_{-\infty}\circ r(A,\Phi)\bigr),

where
A_{0}
is either
A_{X}
or
A_{W}
, and
(\Omega_{X(L)}^{1})_{C}
,
(\Omega_{W(L)}^{1})_{C}
denote the spaces of
1
-forms satisfying
d^{*}(\omega|_{\partial X(L)})=0
and
d^{*}(\omega|_{\partial W(L)})=0
, respectively.
Again, these maps are
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant.

We review the definitions of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant c-stably homotopic maps below.

Let
E_{i}

(i=1,2)
be Hilbert spaces with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-actions.
We denote by
\|\cdot\|_{i}
the norm of
E_{i}
.
Let
\bar{E}_{1}
be the completion of
E_{1}
with respect to a weaker norm,
which we denote by
|\cdot|_{1}
.
We also assume that for any bounded sequence
\{x_{n}\}
in
E_{1}
,
there exists
x_{\infty}\in E_{1}
such that, after passing to a subsequence, we have:

•

\{x_{n}\}
converges to
x_{\infty}
weakly in
E_{1}
,

•

\{x_{n}\}
converges to
x_{\infty}
strongly in
\bar{E}_{1}
.

A pair
L,C\colon E_{1}\to E_{2}
of bounded
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant continuous maps
is called an
admissible pair
if
C
extends to a continuous map

\bar{C}\colon\bar{E}_{1}\to E_{2}
.

Definition 3.27
.

Let
(L,C)
be an admissible pair from
E_{1}
to
E_{2}
, and let

r\colon E_{1}\to V
be a bounded
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant linear map,
where
V
is one of
V(\partial X(L))
or
V(\partial W(L))
,
equipped with the vector fields appearing as the formal gradient
l+c
of the Chern–Simons–Dirac functionals.
We call
(L,C,r)
a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant SWC-triple
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

The map

L\oplus(p^{0}_{-\infty}\circ r)\colon E_{1}\longrightarrow E_{2}\oplus V^{0}_{-\infty}

is Fredholm.

(2)

There exists
M^{\prime}>0
such that for any
x\in E_{1}
satisfying
(L+C)(x)=0
and
a half-trajectory of finite type
\gamma\colon(-\infty,0]\to V
with respect to
l+c
,
with
r(x)=\gamma(0)
, we have

\|x\|_{1}<M^{\prime}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\gamma(t)\|<M^{\prime}

for any
t\geq 0
.

Two
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant SWC-triples
(L_{i},C_{i},r_{i})
for
i=0,1
(with the same domain and target)
are called
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariantly c-homotopic
if there is a homotopy between them through
a continuous family of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant SWC-triples with a uniform constant
M^{\prime}
.

Two
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant SWC-triples
(L_{i},C_{i},r_{i})
for
i=0,1
(with possibly different domains and
targets) are called
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariantly stably c-homotopic
if there exist
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Hilbert spaces
E_{3},E_{4}

such that

\bigl((L_{1}\oplus\mathrm{id}_{E_{3}},\ C_{1}\oplus 0_{E_{3}}),\ r_{1}\oplus 0_{E_{3}}\bigr)

is c-homotopic to

\bigl((L_{2}\oplus\mathrm{id}_{E_{4}},\ C_{2}\oplus 0_{E_{4}}),\ r_{2}\oplus 0_{E_{4}}\bigr).

With these definitions, one can see the following lemma, which is a direct consequence of
[
khandhawit2023unfolded
, Lemma 6.13]
:

Lemma 3.28
.

Let
(L,C)
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant admissible pair from
E_{1}
to
E_{2}
, and let
r\colon E_{1}\to V

be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant linear map. Suppose that we have
a surjective
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant linear map
g\colon E_{1}\to E_{3}
.

Then the triple

(L\oplus g,\ C\oplus 0_{E_{3}},\ r)

is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant SWC-triple if and only if the triple

\bigl(L|_{\ker g},\ C|_{\ker g},\ r|_{\ker g}\bigr)

is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant SWC-triple. In this case, the two triples are
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariantly stably c-homotopic to each other.

We now put

E_{1}=(A_{X}+L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda_{X(L)}^{1})_{CC})\times L^{2}_{k}(S^{+}),\quad E_{2}=iL^{2}_{k-1}(\Lambda_{X(L)}^{+})\times L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-}),\quad V=V(\partial X(L))

and

E_{3}=L^{2}_{k-1}(\Lambda_{X(L)}^{0}),\quad g=d^{*}:(A_{X}+L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda_{X(L)}^{1})_{CC})\times L^{2}_{k}(S^{+})\longrightarrow L^{2}_{k-1}(\Lambda_{X(L)}^{0}).

The maps
L
and
C
are

L=(\rho(d^{+}),{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A_{0}}),C=\mathcal{F}-L.

Then, one can see that all assumptions of
3.28
are satisfied.
By applying
3.28
, we see (
6
) and (
7
) are
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant stably c-homotopic to each other.
Moreover, if two such maps are
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant stably c-homotopic, one can see that the corresponding
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equvariant Bauer–Furuta invariants are also stably
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariantly homotopic. This completes Step 2.

Before proceeding to Step 3, we list the notations that will be used:

\displaystyle Z(L)

\displaystyle=X(L)\sqcup W(L),

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{k}(X(L))

\displaystyle=(A_{X}+L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda_{X(L)}^{1})_{C})\times L^{2}_{k}(S^{+}_{X(L)}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{k}(X(L))

\displaystyle=iL^{2}_{k-1}(\Lambda_{X(L)}^{0}\oplus\Lambda_{X(L)}^{+})\times L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-})\times V(\partial X(L))^{\mu}_{-\infty},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{k}(W(L))

\displaystyle=(A_{W}+L^{2}_{k}(i\Lambda_{W(L)}^{1})_{C})\times L^{2}_{k}(S^{+}_{W(L)}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{k}(W(L))

\displaystyle=iL^{2}_{k-1}(\Lambda_{W(L)}^{0}\oplus\Lambda_{W(L)}^{+})\times L^{2}_{k-1}(S^{-})\times V(\partial W(L))^{\mu}_{-\infty},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{k}(Z(L))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{U}_{k}(X(L))\times\mathcal{U}_{k}(W(L)),

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{k}(Z(L))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{V}_{k}(X(L))\times\mathcal{V}_{k}(W(L)).

Step 3
:
We identify the domain and codomain of the Seiberg–Witten maps for the permuted 4-manifolds. To compare the Seiberg–Witten maps

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{X(L)}+d^{*}+\text{pr}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{U}_{k}(X(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{k}(X(L)),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{W(L)}+d^{*}+\text{pr}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{U}_{k}(W(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{k}(W(L)),

we introduce gluing maps

\displaystyle V^{D}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{U}_{k}(X(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{U}_{k}(W(L)),

\displaystyle V^{C}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{V}_{k}(X(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{k}(W(L)),

which are isomorphisms of Hilbert spaces. To define these maps, we first choose a smooth path

\psi\colon[0,1]\longrightarrow SO(3),

starting at the identity, i.e.
\psi(0)=\mathrm{id}
, and ending at the even permutation, represented by the permutation matrix

\begin{pmatrix}0&1&0\\
0&0&1\\
1&0&0\end{pmatrix}.

A second ingredient in the construction is a smooth function

\gamma\colon[-L,L]\times Y^{\prime}\longrightarrow[0,1],

depending only on the first variable. This function
\gamma
is chosen so that it vanishes on the
[-L,-1]
-part of the neck and is identically
1
on the
[1,L]
-part.
Since the restricted equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on the necks are isomorphic, this homotopy applied to trivializations of bundles gives identifications

\Lambda^{*}_{X(L)}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\Lambda^{*}_{W(L)},\qquad S_{X(L)}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }S_{W(L)}.

This gluing construction, applied to forms
A
and spinors
\Phi
on
Z(L)
, defines a linear map, for which we use the shorthand notation

V_{s}\colon(A,\Phi)\longmapsto(\psi(s)\circ\gamma)\cdot(A,\Phi).

All of these isomorphisms will be denoted collectively by
V
.
They give families of linear isomorphisms

L^{2}_{k}(\Lambda^{*}_{Z(L)})\xrightarrow{V_{s}}L^{2}_{k}(\Lambda^{*}_{Z(L)}),\qquad L^{2}_{k}(S_{Z(L)})\xrightarrow{V_{s}}L^{2}_{k}(S_{Z(L)}),

such that
V_{0}=\mathrm{id}
and
V_{1}
gives the identifications

L^{2}_{k}(\Lambda^{*}_{X(L)})\cong L^{2}_{k}(\Lambda^{*}_{W(L)}),\qquad L^{2}_{k}(S_{X(L)})\cong L^{2}_{k}(S_{W(L)}).

Therefore, applying these to our configuration spaces, we obtain families of automorphisms

\displaystyle V^{D}_{s}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{U}_{k}(Z(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{U}_{k}(Z(L)),

\displaystyle V^{C}_{s}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{V}_{k}(Z(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{k}(Z(L)),

such that
V^{D}_{0}
and
V^{C}_{0}
are the identities, while

V^{D}_{1}=V^{D}
and
V^{C}_{1}=V^{C}
give the identifications

\displaystyle V^{D}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{U}_{k}(X(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{U}_{k}(W(L)),
