\displaystyle V^{C}

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{V}_{k}(X(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{k}(W(L)).

Step 4
:
In this step, we give three homotopies corresponding to
[
bauer2004stable
]
.
We consider

\mathcal{F}_{X(L)}+d^{*}+\text{pr}\ \sqcup\ \mathcal{F}_{W(L)}+d^{*}+\text{pr}

as a map

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{Z(L)}+d^{*}+\text{pr}\;

\displaystyle\colon\mathcal{U}_{k}(Z(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{k}(Z(L)).

Denote by
Y
the boundary
\partial Z(L)
, which is a disjoint union of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
rational homology 3-spheres, and let
A_{0}=A_{X}\sqcup A_{W}
.

For
1\leq R\leq L
, let
\beta_{R}
be a cut-off function

\beta_{R}\colon Z(L)\longrightarrow[0,1]

such that

•

\beta_{R}\equiv 0
on
Z(L)\smallsetminus([-R+1,R-1]\times Y^{\prime})
,

•

\beta_{R}\equiv 1
on
[-R,R]\times Y^{\prime}
,

•

\beta_{R}
depends only on the
[-L,L]
-coordinate.

Set

\beta_{s,R}=(1-s)+s\beta_{R},\qquad s\in[0,1].

We shall use the decomposition of
\mathcal{F}
into the sum
L+C
, where

L=({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{A_{0}}^{+},\,d^{+}).

Consider the following three types of deformations:

(1)

The first homotopy is defined by

\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(1)}\colon\mathcal{U}_{k}(Z(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{k}(Z(L)),\qquad s\in[0,1],

where

C_{s}^{(1)}\begin{pmatrix}a=A-A_{0}\\[4.0pt]
\Phi\end{pmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}-\beta_{L,s}\cdot\rho^{-1}\!\left((\Phi\Phi^{*})_{0}\right)\\[6.0pt]
\rho(a)\Phi\end{bmatrix}.

(2)

The second homotopy is defined by

\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(2)}\colon\mathcal{U}_{k}(Z(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{k}(Z(L)),\qquad s\in[0,1],

where

L_{s}^{(2)}\begin{pmatrix}a=A-A_{0}\\[4.0pt]
\Phi\end{pmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}d^{+}a\\[6.0pt]
D^{+}_{A_{0}}\Phi+\rho(\beta_{L,s}a)\Phi\end{bmatrix}.

(3)

The third homotopy is defined by

\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(3)}=(V_{s}^{C})^{-1}\circ\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{1}^{(2)}+d^{*}+\text{pr}\bigr)\circ V_{s}^{D}\colon\mathcal{U}_{k}(Z(L))\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{k}(Z(L)),\qquad s\in[0,1],

on the necks, and is extended in the obvious way over
Z(T)
.

Note that we do not touch the projection
pr
to the 3-dimensional slice, nor the
d^{*}
-component, while performing these homotopies. From the construction, it is clear that each deformation
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(i)}
is
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant.

For taking finite-dimensional approximations of the above homotopies, it is convenient to introduce the following terminology.
For
s\in[0,1]
and
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, we call a pair

(x,y)\in\mathcal{U}_{k}(Z(L))\times L^{2}_{k}\!\left(i\Lambda^{1}_{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times Y}\oplus S^{+}_{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times Y}\right)

an
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(i)}
–
Z(L)
-trajectory
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

The element
x
is a solution of the deformed Seiberg–Witten equation

\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(i)}(x)=0

on
Z(L)
.

(ii)

The element
y
is a solution of the Seiberg–Witten equation on
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times Y
.

(iii)

The element
y
is in temporal gauge, that is, for each
t
,

d^{*}b(t)=0,

where
y(t)=(b(t),\psi(t))
, and
y
is of finite type.

(iv)

The boundary values match:

x|_{\partial X(L)}=y(0).

Step 5
:
The following estimates will be applied in order to obtain the required homotopies.

(1)

There exist constants
L_{1}
and
R^{(1)}
such that for any
s\in[0,1]
,
L\geq L_{1}
, and any
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(1)}
–
Z(L)
-trajectory, we have

\|x\|_{L^{2}_{k}}<R^{(1)},\qquad\|y(t)\|_{L^{2}_{k-\tfrac{1}{2}}}<R^{(1)}\quad(\forall t\leq 0).

(2)

There exist constants
L_{2}
and
R^{(2)}
such that if
L\geq L_{2}
, the following holds on
Z(L)
:
for any
s\in[0,1]
and any
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(2)}
–
Z(L)
-trajectory with

\|x\|_{L^{2}_{k}}<2R^{(2)},\qquad\|y(t)\|_{L^{2}_{k-\tfrac{1}{2}}}<2R^{(2)}\quad(\forall t\leq 0,\;\forall s\in[0,1]),

we actually have the sharper bounds

\|x\|_{L^{2}_{k}}<R^{(2)},\qquad\|y(t)\|_{L^{2}_{k-\tfrac{1}{2}}}<R^{(2)}\quad(\forall t\leq 0,\;\forall s\in[0,1]).

(3)

There exist constants
L_{3}
and
R^{(3)}
such that if
L\geq L_{3}
, the following holds on
Z(L)
:
for any
\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(3)}
–
Z(L)
-trajectory with

\|x\|_{L^{2}_{k}}<2R^{(3)},\qquad\|y(t)\|_{L^{2}_{k-\tfrac{1}{2}}}<2R^{(3)}\quad(\forall t\leq 0,\;\forall s\in[0,1]),

we obtain the improved bounds

\|x\|_{L^{2}_{k}}<R^{(3)},\qquad\|y(t)\|_{L^{2}_{k-\tfrac{1}{2}}}<R^{(3)}\quad(\forall t\leq 0,\;\forall s\in[0,1]).

These estimates are the “with boundary” versions of those given in
[
bauer2004stable
]
.
Note that Bauer’s original estimates are formulated near the neck and hence do not depend on the presence of additional boundary components.
Thus, by repeating Bauer’s arguments in the neck region, one obtains the desired boundedness properties.

Step 6
:
We obtain a homotopy as the finite-dimensional approximation of the concatenation

\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(3)}*\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(2)}*\mathcal{F}_{s}^{(1)}.

For this purpose, we consider the following criterion. For a subset
A\subset V_{\lambda}^{\mu}(\partial Z(L))
, set

A^{+}:=\{x\in A\mid\forall t>0,\;t\cdot x\in A\}.

Define

R:=\max\{R^{(1)},2R^{(2)},2R^{(3)}\}.

For a small
\epsilon>0
, put

\widetilde{K}_{1}(i):=\bigcup_{s\in[0,1]}\Big(B(R,W_{0})\cap\left(\left(\operatorname{pr}_{W_{1}}\circ\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{s}\right)^{-1}B(\epsilon,W_{1})\right)\Big),

\widetilde{K}_{2}(i):=\bigcup_{s\in[0,1]}\Big(S(R,W_{0})\cap\left(\left(\operatorname{pr}_{W_{1}}\circ\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{s}\right)^{-1}B(\epsilon,W_{1})\right)\Big).

Then,

K_{1}(i):=p_{V^{\mu_{n}}_{\lambda_{n}}}\circ\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{s}(\widetilde{K}_{1}(i)),\qquad K_{2}(i):=p_{V^{\mu_{n}}_{\lambda_{n}}}\circ\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{s}(\widetilde{K}_{2}(i))

satisfy the assumptions of
[
Man03
, Theorem 4]
and
[
Kha15
, Lemma A.4]
for

A:=B(R;V^{\mu}_{\lambda})\subset B(2R;V^{\mu}_{\lambda}).

That is, for any
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, the following conditions hold:

(i)

If
x\in K_{1}(i)\cap A^{+}
, then
([0,\infty)\cdot x)\cap\partial A=\emptyset
.

(ii)

K_{2}(i)\cap A^{+}=\emptyset
.

These conditions ensure that we can take an
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant index pair
(N_{i},L_{i})
of
V(Y)^{\mu}_{\lambda}
, so that there is an induced map

h_{s}(i)\colon\widetilde{K}_{1}(i)/\widetilde{K}_{2}(i)\longrightarrow K_{1}(i)/K_{2}(i)\wedge N_{i}/L_{i}

induced from
\mathcal{F}^{(i)}_{s}
. Moreover, one sees that

h_{0}(1)
and
h_{1}(3)
coincide with

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{1})\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{2})\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{3})\qquad\text{and}\qquad BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{1})\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{2})\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{3}),

respectively. This completes the proof.
∎

We further suppose that
Y^{\prime}
is orientation-preserving diffeomorphic to
L(n,1)
for some
0\neq n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and that the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
Y^{\prime}
is induced by a linear
S^{1}
-action on the total space of the disk bundle
O(n)\to S^{2}
. These actions preserve the positive scalar curvature metric on
L(n,1)
.

Definition 3.29
.

For any element
\mathbf{n}=\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}n_{i}\cdot[i]\in\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
, we associate the
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representation

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{p-1}\mathbb{C}_{[i]}^{\,n_{i}}.

For simplicity, we sometimes abbreviate this as
\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}}
.
Given
\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
(or
\mathbb{Q}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
), we write
\mathbf{m}\geq\mathbf{n}
if
\mathbf{m}-\mathbf{n}
has nonnegative coefficients. These definitions extend naturally to elements of
\mathbb{Q}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
as well.

Before moving on, we recall some facts about
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representations and certain subgroups of
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
For each
k=0,\dots,p-1
, consider the order
p
subgroup

G_{k}=\left\{\left(e^{\frac{2\pi i\ell k}{p}},[\ell]\right)\;\middle|\;\ell\in\mathbb{Z}\right\}\subset S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

It is straightforward to see that

(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}})^{G_{k}}=\mathbb{C}^{n_{k}}.

Lemma 3.30
.

Suppose
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
acts continuously on a topological space
X
. Assume that the induced
S^{1}
-action has no finite stabilizers on
X
, i.e., the only stabilizers are
1
or
S^{1}
. Then for any
x\in X
, the stabilizer of
x
under the
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action is one of the following:

1,\qquad S^{1},\qquad S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p},\qquad G_{k}\ (k=0,\dots,p-1).

Proof.

Let
H\subset S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
be the stabilizer of some point
x\in X
.
If the identity component
S^{1}
is contained in
H
, then since
p
is prime,
H
is either
S^{1}
or
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

Now suppose
S^{1}\cap H=1
. Consider the projection

\varphi\colon H\longrightarrow S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}\xrightarrow{(x,\alpha)\mapsto x}S^{1}.

If
\ker\varphi\neq 1
, then there exists
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\smallsetminus\{0\}
with
(0,\alpha)\in H
, which implies
G_{0}\subset H
.
If, in addition,
(x,\alpha)\in H
for some
(x,\alpha)\notin G_{0}
, then

(x,0)=(x,\alpha)-(0,\alpha)\in H,

contradicting
S^{1}\cap H=1
. Hence in this case
H=G_{0}
.

Thus we may assume
\ker\varphi=1
, i.e.,
\varphi
is injective. Then
\varphi(H)
is a subgroup of order
p
in
S^{1}
.
Hence there exists some
k\in\{0,\dots,p-1\}
such that

\left(e^{\frac{2\pi ik}{p}},[1]\right)\in H.

If another
k^{\prime}\neq k
also satisfies this condition, then

\left(e^{\frac{2\pi i(k^{\prime}-k)}{p}},0\right)\in H,

which contradicts
S^{1}\cap H=1
.
Therefore
k
is unique and
H=G_{k}
, as claimed.
∎

The following gives the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant adjunction relation.

Theorem 3.31
.

Let
X
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}

4
-manifold
(X,\mathfrak{s})
with boundary, which is a disjoint union of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
rational homology
3
-spheres.
Suppose a negatively embedded
2
-sphere
S\subset X
is setwise preserved by the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action. Let
\nu(S)
denote a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant closed neighborhood of
S
.
If we take another
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}
which satisfies

\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}\big|_{X\smallsetminus\overset{\circ}{\nu}(S)}=\mathfrak{s}\big|_{X\smallsetminus\overset{\circ}{\nu}(S)},

then, as
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant stable homotopy classes, we have

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X,\mathfrak{s})=U^{\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{X,\mathfrak{s}}-\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{X,\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}}}\circ BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X,\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}),

where the maps

\begin{split}BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X,\mathfrak{s})&\colon(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{X,\mathfrak{s}})^{+}\longrightarrow(H^{+}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X))^{+}\wedge SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X),\\
BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X,\mathfrak{s}^{\prime})&\colon(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{X,\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}})^{+}\longrightarrow(H^{+}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X))^{+}\wedge SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)\end{split}

are the
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariants of
(X,\mathfrak{s})
and
(X,\mathfrak{s}^{\prime})
, respectively.
For any
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
, the symbol
U^{\mathbf{m}}
denotes the (stable) inclusion
S^{0}\hookrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{m}})^{+}
.

Before proving
3.31
, we state a topological construction that will be used in its proof.

Lemma 3.32
.

Let
n>0
be an integer and consider the lens space
-L(n,1)=L(-n,1)
, which is the boundary of the disk bundle
O(-n)\rightarrow S^{2}
of Euler number
-n
. Endow
O(-n)
with any linear
S^{1}
-action.
5
5

5

See
Section

4.1
for the definition of linear actions on disk bundles.
Then there exists a closed smooth 4-manifold
W
, together with a smooth
S^{1}
-action, that satisfies the following conditions.

•

W
is diffeomorphic to
\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
;

•

O(-n)
embeds
S^{1}
-equivariantly into
W
;

•

Any
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
\partial O(-n)=-L(n,1)
extends to a
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
W
whose
c_{1}
is of the form
(\pm 1,\dots,\pm 1)\in H^{2}(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2};\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

Consider the Hopf link
H
with components
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
.
Performing a
(-n)
-surgery on
H_{1}
yields a knot
H_{2}\subset-L(n,1)
.
Then the smooth
4
-manifold

W_{n-1}=\big(-L(n,1)\times[0,1]\big)\cup(\text{$2$-handle}),

where the
2
-handle is attached along
H_{2}
with
(-1)
-framing relative to the Seifert framing of
H_{2}
in
H
, is a simply connected negative definite cobordism from
-L(n,1)
to
-L(n-1,1)
.
Hence we may form the glued
4
-manifold

W=O(-n)\cup W_{n-1}\cup\cdots\cup W_{1}\cup B,

where
B
is the
4
-ball attached to the
-L(1,1)=S^{3}
boundary component of
W_{1}
.
It is straightforward to see that
W\cong\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
; thus
W
is simply connected and negative definite, and
O(-n)
is smoothly embedded in
W
.
Furthermore, this construction coincides with that used in the proof of
[
kang2024cables
, Lemma 4.12]
.
Consequently, every
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
\partial O(-n)
extends to
W
, and the
c_{1}
of the extended
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure is of the form
(\pm 1,\dots,\pm 1)
.

It remains to prove that the
S^{1}
-action on
O(-n)
extends smoothly to
W
.
To show this, we first prove the following claim:

Given any linear action on
O(-n)
, there exists a smooth
S^{1}
-action on
W_{n-1}
such that the induced actions on
-L(n,1)
coincide, and there exists some linear action on
O(-n+1)
such that the induced actions on
-L(n-1,1)
by
O(-n+1)
and
W_{n-1}
also coincide.

To prove the claim, consider the Hopf link
H
used to construct
W_{n-1}
.
Rotations along each component commute, inducing a smooth
S^{1}\times S^{1}
-action on
S^{3}
that preserves
H
componentwise.
For any
S^{1}
-subaction, we can perform an equivariant surgery along
H_{1}
to obtain an
S^{1}
-action on
-L(n,1)
that fixes
H_{2}
setwise.
It is straightforward to see that these actions are precisely the
S^{1}
-actions on
-L(n,1)
induced by linear actions on
O(-n)
.
Thus, by attaching an equivariant
2
-handle along
H_{2}
, we obtain the desired
S^{1}
-action on
W_{n-1}
, and the induced action on
-L(n-1,1)
arises from some linear action on
O(-n+1)
.
This proves the claim.

Using the claim, we obtain a smooth
S^{1}
-action on
W\smallsetminus B
that extends the given
S^{1}
-action on
O(-n)\subset W\smallsetminus B
.
Furthermore, the restriction of this action to
\partial(W\smallsetminus B)=S^{3}
is induced by some linear action on
O(-1)
.
It is straightforward to see that the list of all possible
S^{1}
-actions on
S^{3}
arising from linear actions on
O(-1)
is as follows:

•

The rotation with respect to an unknotted axis
U\subset S^{3}
.

•

The free action induced by the fiber rotation of
O(-1)
.

In the first case, the action extends to a rotation with respect to a disk-axis
D^{2}\subset B^{4}
, which is evidently smooth.
In the second case, by parametrizing
S^{3}
as the boundary of the unit
4
-ball

B^{4}=\{(z,w)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}\mid|z|^{2}+|w|^{2}=1\},

we see that the
S^{1}
-action on
B^{4}
, defined by

e^{i\theta}\cdot(z,w)=(e^{i\theta}z,\,e^{i\theta}w),

restricts to the given action on
S^{3}
.
Hence, in either case, we obtain a smooth
S^{1}
-action on
B
whose restriction to
\partial B=\partial(W\smallsetminus B)
coincides with the one induced by the
S^{1}
-action on
W\smallsetminus B
.
By gluing them, we obtain a smooth
S^{1}
-action on
W
that extends the given action on
O(-n)
.
The lemma follows.
∎

We also need another lemma concerning the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant index of connected sums of copies of
\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
.

Lemma 3.33
.

Let
n>0
be an integer.
Suppose a smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
fixes a
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\mathfrak{s}
with

c_{1}(\mathfrak{s})=(\pm 1,\dots,\pm 1).

Choose any
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant lift
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
of
\mathfrak{s}
.
Then the
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant index of the
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
Dirac operator

\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})

is zero.

Proof.

Since
\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
is closed, it follows from
3.21
that the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant index of
{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}
lies in
R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
In other words, there exists some
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
such that

\mathrm{ind}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\cong\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}}.

By
3.22
, we obtain the following
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant map:

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\bigl(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}\bigr)\colon(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}})^{+}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{0})^{+}.

It then follows from
4.29
6
6

6

Although this is a forward reference, its proof is elementary, so there is no circular reasoning.
that
\mathbf{n}\leq 0
.

On the other hand, it is straightforward to observe that the
S^{1}
-equivariant index of
{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}}
is
0
.
Hence
|\mathbf{n}|=0
, which implies
\mathbf{n}=0
.
The lemma follows.
∎

Next, we determine all
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariants for any null-homotopic smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
.

Lemma 3.34
.

Let
\mathfrak{s}
be an equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}
.

•

If
c_{1}(\mathfrak{s})=(\pm 1,\dots,\pm 1)
, then

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\sim_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{id}.

•

If
c_{1}(\mathfrak{s})\neq(\pm 1,\dots,\pm 1)
, then

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\sim_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\iota\colon\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}^{\prime}},

where
\mathbf{n}^{\prime}-\mathbf{n}\geq 0
,
\iota
denotes the inclusion, and

\mathbf{n}^{\prime}-\mathbf{n}=\mathrm{ind}^{t}\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\,\mathfrak{s}}\right).

Proof.

In the first case, from
3.33
, we may regard

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\colon\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}}

as a stable
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant map.
For the first claim, namely
BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\sim_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathrm{Id}
, it is sufficient by
[
dieck1987transformation
, page 126, Theorem 4.11]
to check that

\deg\!\left(BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{G}\right)=1

for any subgroup
G\subset S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
appearing as a stabilizer.
Such stabilizers are listed in
3.30
:

1,\quad S^{1},\quad S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p},\quad G_{k}=\left\{\left(e^{\tfrac{2\pi ik\ell}{p}},[\ell]\right)\,\middle|\,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}\right\},\ (k=0,\dots,p-1).

If
G\cap\left(S^{1}\times\{0\}\right)\neq\{1\}
, then
BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{G}
is the compactification of a
\mathbb{C}
-linear isomorphism, hence has degree one.
This covers
S^{1}
,
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and
G_{k}
for
k\neq 0
.
The remaining case is
G_{0}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Here, by the assumptions of
[
dieck1987transformation
, page 126, Theorem 4.11]
, we only need to consider subgroups
G
appearing as stabilizers whose Weyl group
7
7

7

In our context, for a subgroup
H\subset G
, the Weyl group is defined as
W_{G}H=N_{G}H/H
.
is finite.
Since
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is abelian, the normalizer of
G_{0}
is all of
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and therefore

N_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(G_{0})/G_{0}

is infinite.
This completes the proof in the first case.

For the second claim, by
3.22
, we may regard

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\colon\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}^{\prime}}

as a stable
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant map with
\mathbf{n}^{\prime}-\mathbf{n}\geq 0
.
To see that
BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
and
\iota
are
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariantly homotopic, it suffices to check that

\deg\!\left(BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{G}\right)=\deg(\iota)=1

for any subgroup
G\subset S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
appearing as a stabilizer such that

\mathrm{dim}\!\left(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{G}=\mathrm{dim}\!\left(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}^{\prime}}\right)^{G}.

If
G\cap\left(S^{1}\times\{0\}\right)\neq\{1\}
, then by the same reasoning as before we have
\deg\!\left(BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{G}\right)=1
.
For
G_{0}
, we have

\mathrm{dim}\!\left(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}}\right)^{G_{0}}<\mathrm{dim}\!\left(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}^{\prime}}\right)^{G_{0}},

so this case need not be considered.
This completes the proof.
∎

We also need a lemma regarding equivariant metrics of positive scalar curvature on lens spaces.

Lemma 3.35
.

For any integer
n\neq 0
and any finite-order diffeomorphism
\tau
of a lens space
Y=\partial O(-n)
, there exists a
\tau
-equivariant metric of positive scalar curvature on
Y
.

Proof.

The subgroup
\langle\tau\rangle\subset\mathrm{Diff}(Y)
is finite, and
Y
is a spherical space form.
Thus the lemma follows from
[
chow2024equivariant
, Theorem 1.1]
.
∎

Now we prove
3.31
.
For the proof, we fix the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant decomposition of
W
:

W\cong\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2}=O(-n)\cup_{Y^{\prime}}\left(W_{n-1}\cup\cdots\cup W_{1}\cup B\right)=O(-n)\cup_{Y^{\prime}}C,

as obtained from
3.32
.

Proof of
3.31
.

Let
S\subset O(-n)
be a smoothly embedded sphere generating the homology, so that

O(-n)\cup_{Y^{\prime}}C=\nu(S)\cup_{Y^{\prime}}C

gives a decomposition of
W
along
Y^{\prime}=-L(n,1)
.
We put

\displaystyle X_{1,1}

\displaystyle=\left(\nu(S),\mathfrak{s}\right),

\displaystyle X_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\left(\nu(S),\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}\right),

\displaystyle X_{3,1}

\displaystyle=\left(\nu(S),\mathfrak{s}_{0}\right),

\displaystyle X_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\left(X\smallsetminus\overset{\circ}{\nu(S)},\mathfrak{s}\right),

\displaystyle X_{2,2}

\displaystyle=\left(C,\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right),

\displaystyle X_{3,2}

\displaystyle=\left(C,\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right).

where the restrictions of
\mathfrak{s}
and
\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}
are denoted by the same symbols, and
\mathfrak{s}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{s}_{n}
are
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures characterized by the property that the Frøyshov inequality is sharp and the restrictions coincide with
\mathfrak{s}|_{Y}
and
\mathfrak{s}|_{Y^{\prime}}
.

Then we have

\displaystyle X_{1}

\displaystyle=\left(X,\mathfrak{s}\right),

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right),

\displaystyle X_{3}

\displaystyle=\left(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}_{0}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right),

\displaystyle W_{1}

\displaystyle=\left(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right),

\displaystyle W_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}_{0}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right),

\displaystyle W_{3}

\displaystyle=\left(X,\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}\right).

From
3.25
, we obtain

\displaystyle BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(X,\mathfrak{s}\right)\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right)\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}_{0}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right)\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}_{0}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right)\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(X,\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}\right).

By
3.34
, the Bauer–Furuta invariant for
\left(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}_{0}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right)
is stably
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariantly homotopic to the identity:

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}_{0}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right)\sim_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{id}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(X,\mathfrak{s}\right)\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right)=BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right)\wedge BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(X,\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}\right).

Thus it is sufficient to determine

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\left(\#^{n}\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{2},\mathfrak{s}\#\mathfrak{s}_{n}\right)\colon V^{+}\longrightarrow W^{+},

for
V,W\in R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure.
By
3.34
, these maps are canonical inclusions up to stable
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant homotopy.
This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

4.
Equivariant lattice homotopy type

4.1.
S^{1}
-action on plumbed 4-manifolds

Given an integer
n
, consider the disk bundle
p\colon O(n)\to S^{2}
of Euler number
n
.
Choose closed disks
D_{+},D_{-}\subset S^{2}
such that
D_{+}\cup D_{-}=S^{2}
and
D_{+}\cap D_{-}
is a circle.
We also choose trivializations

p|_{p^{-1}(D_{\pm})}\colon p^{-1}(D_{\pm})\cong D^{2}\times D_{\pm}\xrightarrow{(x,y)\mapsto y}D_{\pm}.

Then
p^{-1}(D_{+})
and
p^{-1}(D_{-})
are glued along their boundaries as follows:

p^{-1}(\partial D_{+})\cong D^{2}\times S^{1}\xrightarrow{(z,e^{i\theta})\mapsto(e^{in\theta}z,e^{i\theta})}D^{2}\times S^{1}\cong p^{-1}(\partial D_{-}).

Thus, to construct an
S^{1}
-action on
O(n)
, it suffices to define
S^{1}
-actions on
p^{-1}(D_{\pm})
and verify that, when restricted to
\partial D_{\pm}
, the action commutes with the gluing map.
In this subsection, we describe
S^{1}
-actions on
O(n)
arising in this way and explain their relation to Seifert fibered spaces.

First consider the
S^{1}
-action on
p^{-1}(D_{\pm})
defined by

e^{i\theta}\cdot(z,w)=\left(e^{i\theta}z,w\right),\quad(z,w)\in D^{2}\times D_{\pm}.

This action clearly commutes with the gluing map and therefore defines a smooth
S^{1}
-action on the total space
O(n)
.
We call this the
fiber rotation
(or
fiberwise rotation
).

In general, for any pair of parameters
(p_{+},q_{+}),(p_{-},q_{-})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\smallsetminus\{(0,0)\}
, we may consider the action

e^{i\theta}\cdot(z,w)=\left(e^{ip_{\pm}\theta}z,\,e^{iq_{\pm}\theta}w\right),\qquad(z,w)\in D^{2}\times D_{\pm}.

This action commutes with the gluing map if and only if the following linear relation holds:

\begin{pmatrix}p_{-}\\
q_{-}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&n\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}p_{+}\\
q_{+}\end{pmatrix}.

We call the
S^{1}
-actions on
O(n)
arising in this way
linear actions
.
More generally, we call any self-diffeomorphisms of
O(n)
arising in this way
linear diffeomorphisms
.
Note that all linear actions on
O(n)
preserve the zero-section of
p
setwise.
Clearly, the fiberwise rotation is a linear action.

Now let
Y
be a Seifert fibered rational homology sphere.
Then there exists a unique star-shaped negative definite almost rational plumbing graph
\Gamma
such that
Y\cong W_{\Gamma}
.
Note that
W_{\Gamma}
is obtained by gluing disk bundles, i.e.,

W_{\Gamma}=\bigcup_{v\in V(\Gamma)}D_{v}.

We endow the disk bundle
D_{v_{c}}
associated to the central node
v_{c}
of
\Gamma
with the fiberwise rotation.
Then Orlik
[
Orlik:1972-1
, Section 2, Corollary 5]
showed that there exist unique linear actions on
D_{v}
for each
v\in V(\Gamma)\smallsetminus\{v_{c}\}
such that they glue together to a well-defined smooth
S^{1}
-action on the entire
4
-manifold
W_{\Gamma}
.
Furthermore, the induced
S^{1}
-action on
Y=\partial W_{\Gamma}
is fixed-point-free and coincides with the Seifert action of
Y
.

4.2.
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures

Choose any prime
p
. We first confirm the definition of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Spin
c
structures.

Definition 4.1
.

Given a smooth oriented
n
-manifold
X
together with a smooth left
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action
\tau
preserving the orientation, a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
X
consists of a
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\mathfrak{s}
on
X
, together with a smooth lift of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on the frame bundle of
X
to the principal
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
bundle
P(\mathfrak{s})
of
\mathfrak{s}
, i.e., a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}P(\mathfrak{s})@>{\tilde{\tau}}>{}>P(\mathfrak{s})\\
@V{\pi}V{}V@V{\pi}V{}V\\
\mathrm{Fr}(X)@>{\tau_{*}}>{}>\mathrm{Fr}(X)\end{CD}\qquad\text{with}\quad\tilde{\tau}^{p}=\mathrm{Id},

where
\mathrm{Fr}(X)
denotes the frame bundle with respect to a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant Riemannian metric
g
that is a product near the boundary, and
\tau_{*}
denotes the induced action on
\mathrm{Fr}(X)
so that
\tilde{\tau}
commutes with the right
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(n)
-action.

Isomorphisms of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures are defined in the obvious way. We describe how to classify
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Spin
c
structures, using equivariant classifying spaces.
The following remark reviews the properties of equivariant classifying spaces:

Remark 4.2
.

We explain the general theory of equivariant classifying spaces; see
[
husemoller1966fibre
,
lashof1982equivariant
,
lashof1986generalized
,
may1990some
]
for more details.
Let
X
be a left
G
-CW complex for a compact Lie group
G
. We note that the definition of
G
-equivariant principal
H
-bundles depends on an extension of
G
by
H
as compact Lie groups:

\{e\}\to H\to\Gamma\to G\to\{e\},

where
H
is a normal closed subgroup of
\Gamma
. Then, for a fixed extension, the general notion of
G
-equivariant principal
H
-bundles is defined as follows: for a given left
G
-space
X
, a principal
(H;\Gamma)
-bundle
(P,\pi)
is a principal
H
-bundle
\pi:P\to X
with the left
\Gamma
-action such that

•

the left action of
\Gamma
and the right action of
H
has the relation:

\gamma\cdot(p\cdot h)\;=\;(\gamma\cdot p)\cdot(\gamma h\gamma^{-1}),\qquad(\gamma\in\Gamma,\;h\in H,\;p\in P)\text{ and }

•

the diagram

\begin{CD}\Gamma\times P@>{}>{}>P\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
G\times X@>{}>{}>X\end{CD}

commutes.

Note that when
\Gamma=H\times G
, the first equation becomes

\gamma\cdot(p\cdot h)\;=\;(\gamma\cdot p)\cdot h

which is equivalent to say that the left
G
-action and the right
H
-action commute.
The isomorphisms of principal
(H;\Gamma)
-bundles are defined naturally. We denote by
\operatorname{Pr}^{(H;\Gamma)}(X)
the set of isomorphisms classes of principal
(H;\Gamma)
-bundles for the extension.
In
[
lashof1982equivariant
,
may1990some
]
, Lashof and May constructed a a
G
-space
B_{G}^{\Gamma}H
togather with the universal principal
(H;\Gamma)

E_{G}^{\Gamma}H\to B_{G}^{\Gamma}H

and with a natural bijection:

[X,B_{G}^{\Gamma}H]^{G}\cong\operatorname{Pr}^{(H;\Gamma)}(X),

where the left hand side is the set of
G
-equivariant homotopy classes of
G
-maps between
X
and
B_{G}^{\Gamma}H
. This
G
-space
B_{G}^{\Gamma}H
is called equivariant classifying space for
(H;\Gamma)
. When we take the extension as the product
\Gamma=H\times G
, we simply denote
B_{G}^{\Gamma}H
by
B_{G}H
. In this paper, we only use the product case. Here we summarize its construction in the case of
\Gamma=H\times G
:
Choose a representative subgroup
S
from each conjugacy class of closed subgroups of
G
.
For each such
S
, fix a representative homomorphism

p\colon S\longrightarrow H

from each
H
-equivalence class of homomorphisms
(two homomorphisms being equivalent if they are conjugate in
H
).
Let
\{p_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\Lambda}
denote the resulting collection of representatives.

For each pair
(S,p_{\alpha})
, define

E_{\alpha}\;=\;G\times_{S}H,

where
S
acts on
H
via
p_{\alpha}
, i.e.
s\cdot h:=p_{\alpha}(s)h
.
Set

E=\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in\Lambda}E_{\alpha}.

Following
[
husemoller1966fibre
, Section 11]
, one now forms the infinite join

E_{G}H\;=\;E^{*\infty}=E*E*E*\cdots,

which inherits a natural structure of a
G
-equivariant principal
H
-bundle.
The equivariant classifying space is defined to be the base

B_{G}H:=E_{G}H/H

equipped with a natural
G
-action.

Using it, a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Spin
c
structure corresponds to a homotopy class of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant lifts of a given
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant map
X\to B_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}SO(n)
to a map
X\to B_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(n)
. Since there is a fiber sequence

B_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}U(1)\longrightarrow B_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathrm{Spin}(n)\longrightarrow B_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}SO(n),

such lifts are classified by elements of
[X,B_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}U(1)]^{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, the set of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant homotopy classes of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant maps.

We denote by
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)
the set of
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
X
, and by
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
the set of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
X
.
From the discussion above, we obtain natural bijections

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)\cong[X,BU(1)]\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\cong[X,B_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}U(1)]^{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},

where
[X,BU(1)]
denotes the set of homotopy classes of maps
X\to BU(1)
, the classifying space of
U(1)
.
Equivalently, we claim that the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant Spin
c
structures are classified by
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant principal
U(1)
-bundles.

Given a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\mathfrak{s}
on
X
, we denote its underlying
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
X
by
\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{s})
.
We first consider the case where
X
is a disk bundle over
S^{2}
endowed with a linear
S^{1}
-action, and we take the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
X
to be the corresponding subaction.
Let
S_{X}
denote the zero-section of
X
; note that
S_{X}
is setwise
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant, and the induced
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
S_{X}
is either trivial or a rotation.
Since
X
admits a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant deformation retraction onto
S_{X}
, we obtain

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)\cong[S_{X},BU(1)]\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\cong[S_{X},B_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}U(1)]^{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}.

Because
U(1)
is
1
-dimensional, it follows from
[
rezk2018classifying
, Corollary 1.6]
that

[S_{X},B_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}U(1)]^{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\cong[S_{X}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}E\mathbb{Z}_{p},BU(1)].

Hence we deduce the (uncanonical) identifications
8
8

8

Using the same argument, one can show that
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\cong H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X;\mathbb{Z})
even when
X
is not a disk bundle.

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)\cong H^{2}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\cong H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z}),

which fit into the following commutative square, where the bottom map is the canonical map from equivariant to ordinary cohomology:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z}).}

To compute
H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z})
, we use the Serre spectral sequence

E_{2}^{i,j}=H^{i}(B\mathbb{Z}_{p};H^{j}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z}))\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;H^{i+j}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X;\mathbb{Z}).

Since the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
S_{X}
is orientation-preserving, the local system
H^{j}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z})
is trivial over
B\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, so the spectral sequence reduces to

E_{2}^{i,j}=H^{i}(B\mathbb{Z}_{p};\mathbb{Z})\otimes H^{j}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z})\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;H^{i+j}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X;\mathbb{Z}).

Because
E_{2}^{i,j}=0
whenever either
i
or
j
is odd, there can be no nontrivial differential
d_{n}
for
n\geq 2
, so the spectral sequence degenerates at
E_{2}
.
Thus we obtain a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{2}(B\mathbb{Z}_{p};\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow H^{2}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z})\;(=\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow 0.

Since
H^{2}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z})
is free, the sequence splits, yielding the following lemma.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
X
be a disk bundle over
S^{2}
equipped with a linear
S^{1}
-action, and let
X
carry the restricted
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-subaction.
Then there is a natural bijection

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\xrightarrow{\;\;\cong\;\;}\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p},

where the first coordinate is the underlying nonequivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
X
.

As an immediate consequence we obtain:

Corollary 4.4
.

Under the assumptions of
4.3
, let
U\subset X
be a contractible open subset that is setwise
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant.
Suppose
\mathfrak{s}_{X}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{U}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(U)
satisfy

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{U})=\mathfrak{s}_{X}|_{U}.

Then there exists a unique
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
such that

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X})=\mathfrak{s}_{X}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X}|_{U}=\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{U}.

On the fixed point set
X^{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
with a fixed orientation, the framed bundle
\mathrm{Fr}(X)
admits a reduction to a principal

T=U(1)\times U(1)\;\subset\;SO(4)

bundle
P(T)
, arising from the identification
T_{x}X\cong\mathbb{C}^{2}
with the induced
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representation.
Such a reduction is unique up to homotopy.
Correspondingly, the principal
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
bundle
P(\mathfrak{s})
admits a reduction to a principal
\tilde{T}
-bundle
P(\tilde{T})
, where

\tilde{T}=U(1)\times U(1)\times U(1)\,/\,\{\pm(1,1,1)\}\;\subset\;\mathrm{Sp}(1)\times\mathrm{Sp}(1)\times U(1)\,/\,\{\pm(1,1,1)\}.

The projection
P(\tilde{T})_{x}\to P(T)_{x}
is given by

[(x,y,z)]\longmapsto(xy,\,xy^{-1}).

Since the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action is orientation-preserving, every component of
X^{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
has even codimension.
In particular, the connected component
S
of
X^{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
containing
x
has codimension
2
or
4
.

Suppose first that
S
has codimension
2
.
Then, near
x
, the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is locally modeled by the fiber rotation of the normal bundle of
S
.
We may write the fiber rotation angle of the action of
[1]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
as
\tfrac{2k\pi}{p}
, where
0<k<p
.
The induced action on
P(\tilde{T})_{x}
is then

(x,y)\longmapsto(x,\,\zeta_{p}^{k}y),\qquad x,y\in\mathbb{C}.

All possible
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lifts can be listed as

(8)

[(x,y,z)]\longmapsto\left[\big((-1)^{k+1}\zeta_{2p}^{\,k}x,\;(-1)^{k+1}\zeta_{2p}^{\,-k}y,\;\zeta_{p}^{\,m}\,\zeta_{2p}z\big)\right],\qquad m\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

Now suppose that
S
has codimension
4
, i.e.
x\in X^{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is an isolated fixed point.
Then, near
x
, the action of
[1]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
can locally be written as

(x,y)\longmapsto\big(\zeta_{p}^{k_{1}}x,\;\zeta_{p}^{k_{2}}y\big),\qquad x,y\in\mathbb{C}.

All possible
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lifts can then be listed as

(9)

[(x,y,z)]\longmapsto\left[\big((-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}+1}\zeta_{2p}^{\,k_{1}+k_{2}}x,\;(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}+1}\zeta_{2p}^{\,k_{1}-k_{2}}y,\;\zeta_{p}^{\,m}\,\zeta_{2p}z\big)\right],\qquad m\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

Definition 4.5
.

We define the number
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in

Equations

8
and
9

as the
equivariance number

n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\mathfrak{s})
of
\mathfrak{s}
at
x
.

It is straightforward to check that the value of
n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv}}
depends only on the connected component of
X^{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
containing
x
and its orientation. Hence we fix orientations on each component of
X^{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
from now on. Also, if
X^{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is connected, we will often drop
x
from the notation and simply write
n_{\mathrm{eqv}}
.

Remark 4.6
.

When
p
is odd, the equivariance number can alternatively be defined as follows: the generator
[1]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
acts on the fiber of the determinant line bundle of
\mathfrak{s}
by a
\tfrac{4\pi}{p}
-rotation. This description, however, does not apply when
p=2
, since it requires
2
to be invertible modulo
p
. For this reason we used the local model definition, which works uniformly for all primes
p
.

To show that
n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv}}
is indeed a projection

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p},

it suffices to prove that the restriction

n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv},\,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}\colon\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))\xrightarrow{\;\cong\;}\mathbb{Z}_{p},

is a bijection, where
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))
denotes the subset of
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
whose nonequivariant truncation is
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
.

For this, consider the
twisting operation
on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures. Recall that such a structure is given by a principal

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(4)=\mathrm{Spin}(4)\times_{\{\pm 1\}}U(1)

bundle
E\to X
inducing the tangent bundle of
X
, together with a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
E
that lifts the given action on
X
. For any
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we may modify this
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action by multiplying the action of
[1]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with
e^{2\pi ik/p}\in U(1)
. We call this operation the
k
-twisting
. Note that
k
-twisting is well-defined for any smooth manifold equipped with a smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action.
By definition of
n_{\mathrm{eqv}}
, if
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k}
denotes the
k
-twist of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
, then

n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k})=n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})+[k]\quad(\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Since
k
-twisting does not change the nonequivariant truncation, i.e.,
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
, it follows that
n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv},\,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}
is surjective. On the other hand,
4.3
shows that

\bigl|\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))\bigr|=|\mathbb{Z}_{p}|=p,

so
n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv},\,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}
must also be injective. Therefore it is a bijection, and
n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv}}
has the desired properties. We may summarize this as follows.

Lemma 4.7
.

Under the assumptions of
4.3
, for every
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-fixed point
x\in X
, the assignment

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\cong\;\;}\;\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p};\qquad\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}\longmapsto\bigl(\,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}),\,n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})\,\bigr)

is a bijection.

Then the following corollary is immediate.

Corollary 4.8
.

Under the assumptions of
4.3
, for any
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
satisfying
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})
, there exists a unique element
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}
is obtained by
k
-twisting
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
.

Proof.

Since
n_{\mathrm{eqv}}
is well-defined up to an overall cyclic permutation of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, the difference
n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})-n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
determines a well-defined element of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, which we denote by
[k]
. Denote the
k
-twisting of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
by
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k}
. Then we have

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}),\qquad n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k})=n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})+[k]=n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}),

so the corollary follows from
4.7
.
∎

We call the number
n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
the
equivariance number
of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
(at
x
). This value depends on
x
, but one easily checks that, for any other
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-fixed point
x^{\prime}
of
X
, there exists a constant
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that

n^{x^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})=n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})+\alpha\qquad\text{for all}\quad\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X).

Hence, in many cases, we will simply drop
x
from the notation and treat
n_{\mathrm{eqv}}
as a function well-defined up to an overall cyclic permutation in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

Remark 4.9
.

When the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
X
is induced by the fiberwise rotation, the fixed point set is the zero section which is connected. Hence the value of
n^{x}_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
does not depend on the choice of a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-fixed point
x\in X
. In this case, we also drop
x
from the notation and say that
n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
is a well-defined element of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

Observe that, instead of using
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-fixed points, we may also use setwise
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant open contractible subsets of
X
to detect the equivariance number. The caveat is that we can detect it only up to an overall cyclic permutation of elements of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, since in the general case
n_{\mathrm{eqv}}
is well-defined only modulo such an ambiguity. Nevertheless, this is still sufficient to prove the following lemma.

Lemma 4.10
.

Under the assumptions of
4.3
, let
U\subset X
be a contractible open subset that is setwise
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant. Suppose that two
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
agree on
U
. Then

n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{1})=n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}).

Proof.

The map
n_{\mathrm{eqv}}
can be interpreted as the equivariant pullback

H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X;\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(U;\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}

induced by the inclusion
U\hookrightarrow X
. Since equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
U
are classified by elements of
H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(U;\mathbb{Z})
, the agreement of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}
on
U
forces their images under this pullback to coincide, giving the desired equality.
∎

We now consider the following relative lifting problem. Let
X
be a disk bundle over
S^{2}
equipped with a linear
S^{1}
-action that acts trivially on the zero-section
S_{X}\subset X
. Endow
X
with the induced
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-subaction. Choose
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)
and
\mathfrak{s}_{X}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)
such that

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial})=\mathfrak{s}_{X}|_{\partial X}.

The problem is to determine how many elements
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
satisfy both

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X})=\mathfrak{s}_{X}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X}|_{\partial X}=\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}\;.

To tackle this problem, we first analyze the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant boundary restriction map

\mathrm{res}^{\partial}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\colon\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X).

From the preceding discussion, we obtain natural identifications

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\cong H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X;\mathbb{Z})\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)\cong H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X;\mathbb{Z}),

which fit into the following commutative square, where
i_{\partial X}
denotes the inclusion
\partial X\hookrightarrow X
:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{res}^{\partial}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X;\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\partial X}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X;\mathbb{Z}).}

Since
X
is a disk bundle over
S^{2}
, its boundary
\partial X
is a lens space. If the Euler number of
X
is
n
, then
\partial X\cong L(n,1)
. Moreover, the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
\partial X
is free, with quotient
L(np,1)
, while
X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is a disk bundle of Euler number
np
over
S^{2}
.
To compute
i_{\partial X}^{\ast}
, we consider the following commutative diagram. Here the vertical maps are induced by the natural collapsing maps
Y\times_{G}EG\to Y/G
, and
i_{\partial X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\ast}
denotes the inclusion
\partial X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\hookrightarrow X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Note also that, since the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action is trivial on the zero-section
S_{X}
, we have
S_{X}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}=S_{X}
.

\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{1\mapsto[1]}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{np}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{2}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{H^{2}(S_{X}/\mathbb{Z}_{p};\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{2}(X/\mathbb{Z}_{p};\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{i_{\partial X/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{H^{2}(\partial X/\mathbb{Z}_{p};\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{2}(S_{X}\times B\mathbb{Z}_{p};\mathbb{Z})}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(S_{X};\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X;\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{i_{\partial X}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X;\mathbb{Z})}

Since the projection pullback
\mathrm{pr}^{\ast}
is given by

\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id},0)}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p},

it follows that, if we write
i_{\partial X}^{\ast}
as

i^{\ast}_{\partial X}:\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{np},

then it satisfies
i^{\ast}_{\partial X}(1,[0])=1
.

It is clear that, for any
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, the
k
-twisting map

\mathrm{tw}^{X}_{k}\colon\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}

is given by

\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}\xrightarrow{(i,[j])\mapsto(i,[j+k])}\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

On the boundary
\partial X=L(n,1)
, the
k
-twisting map

\mathrm{tw}^{\partial X}_{k}\colon\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)

is described by

\mathbb{Z}_{np}\xrightarrow{[i]\mapsto[i+p]}\mathbb{Z}_{np}.

Since the
k
-twisting operation clearly commutes with restrictions to setwise
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant submanifolds, we compute (with slight abuse of notation):

i^{\ast}_{\partial X}(0,[k])=\mathrm{res}^{\partial}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathrm{tw}_{k}(0,[0]))=\mathrm{tw}_{k}(\mathrm{res}^{\partial}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(0,[0]))=i^{\ast}_{\partial X}(0,[0])+[kn].

We therefore deduce the following lemma.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
X
be a disk bundle of Euler number
n
over
S^{2}
, equipped with a smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action by fiberwise rotation. Then, under the identifications

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\cong\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)\cong\mathbb{Z}_{np},

the equivariant restriction map

\mathrm{res}^{\partial}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\colon\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)

is given by

\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}\xrightarrow{(i,[j])\mapsto[i+nj]}\mathbb{Z}_{np}.

Using
4.3
and
4.11
, we now state a lemma that completely resolves the equivariant relative lifting problem discussed earlier.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
X
be a disk bundle over
S^{2}
, equipped with a smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action by fiberwise rotation.
For any
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)
and
\mathfrak{s}_{X}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)
with

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial})=\mathfrak{s}_{X}|_{\partial X},

there exists a unique
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
such that

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X})=\mathfrak{s}_{X}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X}|_{\partial X}=\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}.

Proof.

By
4.3
and
4.11
, under the identifications

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\cong\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p},\qquad\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)\cong\mathbb{Z},\qquad\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)\cong\mathbb{Z}_{np},

the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant boundary restriction map
\mathrm{res}^{\partial}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, the non-equivariant boundary restriction map
\mathrm{res}^{\partial}
, and the forgetful maps
\mathcal{N}
are given by the following diagram:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{res}^{\partial}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{N}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{N}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(i,[j])\mapsto[i+nj]}
\scriptstyle{(i,[j])\mapsto i}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{np}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[i]\mapsto[i]}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i\mapsto[i]}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{n}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{res}^{\partial}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(\partial X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

Now choose
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)
and
\mathfrak{s}_{X}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)
such that
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial})=\mathfrak{s}_{X}|_{\partial X}
.
Suppose
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}
corresponds to
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{np}
and
\mathfrak{s}_{X}
corresponds to
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The compatibility condition becomes

[k]=[\ell]\in\mathbb{Z}_{n},\qquad\text{i.e.,}\quad k\equiv\ell\pmod{n}.

Thus
k-\ell
is a multiple of
n
, and
\left[\tfrac{k-\ell}{n}\right]
defines an element of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Therefore, there exists a unique
(i,[j])\in\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that

[i+nj]=[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{np}
and
i=\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The unique solution is

i=\ell\qquad\text{ and }\qquad[j]=\left[\frac{k-\ell}{n}\right].

This proves the claim.
∎

We also need a similar lemma for disk bundles over
S^{2}
with arbitrary linear actions.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
X
be a disk bundle over
S^{2}
, where
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
acts as a subaction of some linear
S^{1}
-action on
X
.
For any
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial X)
and
\mathfrak{s}_{X}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)
with

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial})=\mathfrak{s}_{X}|_{\partial X},

there exists a unique
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)
such that

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X})=\mathfrak{s}_{X}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{X}|_{\partial X}=\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}.

Proof.

Consider the space

X^{\prime}=\mathrm{Cone}(\partial X\hookrightarrow X).

Since
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}
admits an extension to
X
, we see that
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
X
that restrict to
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}
on
\partial X
are classified by elements of
H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X^{\prime};\mathbb{Z})
. Similarly, nonequivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
X
that restrict to
\mathfrak{s}_{X}|_{\partial X}=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial})
are classified by elements of
H^{2}(X^{\prime};\mathbb{Z})
. Hence it suffices to show that the natural map

H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X^{\prime};\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow H^{2}(X^{\prime};\mathbb{Z})

is an isomorphism. To see this, observe that
X^{\prime}
is the Thom space of
X
. Hence we have the following commutative diagram, where the vertical maps are Thom isomorphisms:

\textstyle{H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X^{\prime};\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{2}(X^{\prime};\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{0}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X;\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(X;\mathbb{Z})}

The bottom horizontal map is clearly an isomorphism. The lemma follows.
∎

We now consider the same question for
W_{\Gamma}
for a very special class of plumbing graphs
\Gamma
.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
\Gamma
be a star-shaped negative definite almost rational plumbing graph, so that
S^{1}
acts on
W_{\Gamma}
as discussed in
Section

4.1
, restricting to the Seifert action on the rational homology sphere
Y=\partial W_{\Gamma}
. Let
p
be a prime that does not divide
|H_{1}(Y;\mathbb{Z})|
. Then for any
\mathfrak{s}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(W_{\Gamma})
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)
satisfying

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial})=\mathfrak{s}|_{Y},

there exists a unique
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma})
such that

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})=\mathfrak{s}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}|_{Y}=\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\partial}.

Proof.

As in the proof of
4.3
, since
H^{\ast}(W_{\Gamma};\mathbb{Z})
is supported only in even degrees, we have a canonical bijection

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(W_{\Gamma})\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

To compute
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)\cong H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y;\mathbb{Z})
, consider the Serre spectral sequence

E_{2}^{i,j}=H^{i}(B\mathbb{Z}_{p};H^{j}(Y;\mathbb{Z}))\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;H^{i+j}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y;\mathbb{Z}).

Since
H^{2}(Y;\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}=0
, the
E_{2}
page takes the following form:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 3}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2}(Y;\mathbb{Z})}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{p}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}

The bottom and top rows must cancel against each other, which occurs via a nontrivial
d_{4}
differential (indicated in red). Thus the sequence degenerates at the
E_{5}
page, yielding a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}\longrightarrow H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y;\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow H^{2}(Y;\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow 0.

Since
p
does not divide
|H^{2}(Y;\mathbb{Z})|
, this sequence splits. Therefore,

H^{2}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y;\mathbb{Z})\cong H^{2}(Y;\mathbb{Z})\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p},

giving a canonical (up to cyclic shift of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
) bijection

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)\;\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\;\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(Y)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

As every
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
Y
extends to
W_{\Gamma}
