This follows from the discussion of
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
W_{\Gamma}
and
Y
in
Section

2.2
.
, the same argument as in
4.13
proves the claim.
∎

Observe that under the assumptions of
4.14
, for any
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma})
the element

n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}|_{D_{v_{c}}})\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}

is well-defined, where
D_{v_{c}}
denotes the disk bundle corresponding to the central node of
\Gamma
. Indeed, the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
W_{\Gamma}
restricts to fiber rotation on
D_{v_{c}}
. We will abuse notation and write this value as
n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
, referring to it as the
equivariance number
of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
.

Lemma 4.15
.

Under the assumptions of
4.14
, the map

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(W_{\Gamma})\times\mathbb{Z}_{p};\qquad\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}\longmapsto\bigl(\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}),\,n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})\bigr)

is a bijection. Moreover, if
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma})
satisfy

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})
, then there exists a unique
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that

\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}
is the
k
-twist of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
.

Proof.

We follow the arguments in the proof of
4.7
.
For a given
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
, let

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))\;\subset\;\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma})

denote the subset of elements whose nonequivariant truncation equals
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
.
By
4.15
, the restriction

n_{\mathrm{eqv},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}:\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p}

is surjective, since
k
-twisting changes its value by
[k]
.
Furthermore, the proof of
4.14
shows that

\bigl|\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))\bigr|=|\mathbb{Z}_{p}|=p.

Surjectivity together with this cardinality count implies bijectivity, completing the proof.
∎

As a corollary, we obtain a similar statement for
Y=\partial W_{\Gamma}
.

Corollary 4.16
.

Suppose that
|H_{1}(Y;\mathbb{Z})|
is not divisible by
p
.
Then, for any
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)
with

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})
,
there exists a unique
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}
is the
k
-twisting of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
.

Proof.

Since every
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
Y
extends to
W_{\Gamma}
, it follows from
4.14
that there exists some
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma})
with
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma}|_{Y}=\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
. Using
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma}
, we define a map

F\colon\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{p},

where
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))\subset\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)
denotes the subset of elements whose nonequivariant truncation is
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
.
Given any
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))
, by
4.14
, there exists a unique
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{\Gamma}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma})
such that
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{\Gamma})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma})
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{\Gamma}|_{Y}=\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}
. We then set

F(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})=\bigl(n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{\Gamma})-n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma})\bigr)\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

Next, define a map

\mathrm{Tw}:\mathbb{Z}_{p}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))

by declaring
\mathrm{Tw}([k])
to be the
k
-twisting of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
. To prove the claim, it suffices to show that
\mathrm{Tw}
is bijective.
Consider the composition
F\circ\mathrm{Tw}
. For any
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, let
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{k}_{\Gamma}
denote the
k
-twisting of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma}
. By definition of
k
-twisting, we have
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{k}_{\Gamma}|_{Y}
equal to the
k
-twisting of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
. Thus,

F(\mathrm{Tw}([k]))=n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{k}_{\Gamma})-n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma})=[k],

so that
F\circ\mathrm{Tw}=\mathrm{id}
. Hence
\mathrm{Tw}
is injective.
Finally, by the proof of
4.14
, we have

\bigl|\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))\bigr|=|\mathbb{Z}_{p}|=p.

Injectivity together with this cardinality argument shows that
\mathrm{Tw}
is bijective, completing the proof.
∎

This corollary has striking consequences.

Lemma 4.17
.

Suppose that
|H_{1}(Y;\mathbb{Z})|
is not divisible by
p
.
Then, for any
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y)
with

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})
,
there exists a unique
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that

SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})

and

SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})
are

(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariantly homotopy equivalent,
after reparametrizing the
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on

SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
by the automorphism

S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}\longrightarrow S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p};\qquad(x,[n])\longmapsto\left(e^{\tfrac{2\pi ikn}{p}}x,[n]\right).

Proof.

By
4.16
, there exists a unique
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
such that

\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}
is the
k
-twisting of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
.
Since
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})
,
the underlying spinor spaces, together with their
S^{1}
-actions, coincide.
The difference lies in the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-actions, which are related exactly by the reparametrization described in the lemma.
∎

Lemma 4.18
.

Suppose that
|H_{1}(Y;\mathbb{Z})|
is not divisible by
p
.
Then the map

\mathcal{S}_{Y,\mathfrak{s}}\colon\mathrm{Split}(Y,\mathfrak{s})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s})

is bijective for any
\mathfrak{s}\in\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(Y)
.

Proof.

Choose any section
f\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to G_{\mathfrak{s}}
of the central extension

1\longrightarrow S^{1}\longrightarrow G_{\mathfrak{s}}\xrightarrow{\;\varphi\;}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\longrightarrow 1,

and denote the corresponding
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure

\mathcal{S}_{Y,\mathfrak{s}}(f)
by
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
.
Using
f
, we identify
G_{\mathfrak{s}}\cong S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
so that

f([m])=(0,[m])
and
\varphi(x,[m])=[m]
.
For each
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, define

f_{k}([m])=\left(e^{\tfrac{2\pi ikm}{p}},[m]\right)\in S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}\cong G_{\mathfrak{s}}.

Then
f_{k}
is also a section of
\varphi
, and in fact every section arises uniquely in this way.
Since
\mathcal{S}_{Y,\mathfrak{s}}(f_{k})
is the
k
-twist of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
, the image of
\mathcal{S}_{Y,\mathfrak{s}}
is precisely the set of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures obtained from
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
via
k
-twisting,
[k]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
By
4.16
, this set equals the entire
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s})
.
Hence
\mathcal{S}_{Y,\mathfrak{s}}
is surjective.
Finally, since
|\mathrm{Split}(Y,\mathfrak{s})|=p=|\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(Y,\mathfrak{s})|
, surjectivity implies bijectivity.
This proves the claim.
∎

We also consider the case
p=2
, where we deal with self-conjugate

\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant lifts of self-conjugate
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
Y
.
Note that Spin structures on
Y
are in natural bijection with self-conjugate

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
Y
. We recall and define:

•

the set
\mathrm{Spin}(Y)
of Spin structures on
Y
;

•

the set
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(Y)
of
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
Y
;

•

the set
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(Y)_{0}
of self-conjugate
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
Y
;

•

the set
\mathrm{Spin}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)
of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant Spin structures on
Y
;

•

the set
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)
of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
Y
;

•

the set
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)_{0}
of self-conjugate
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
Y
.

Here, a self-conjugate
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
is defined as follows.

Definition 4.19
.

Let
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}=(P,\tau)
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure.
We define its
conjugate

\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure by

\overline{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}:=\left(\overline{P},\overline{\tau}\right),

where
\overline{P}
is the principal
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(n)
-bundle obtained from
P
by
extension of structure group along the conjugation map

(a,b)\longmapsto(a,\overline{b})\colon\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(n)=\mathrm{Spin}(n)\times_{\{\pm 1\}}U(1)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spin}(n)\times_{\{\pm 1\}}U(1)=\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(n).

The induced
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-lift
\overline{\tau}
arises from the natural identification

P\cong\overline{P}
.

Observe that, since Spin structures can be naturally regarded as
self-conjugate
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures via the inclusion

\mathrm{Spin}(n)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(n)
, we obtain canonical maps

\mathcal{F}_{Y}\colon\mathrm{Spin}(Y)\xrightarrow{\;\cong\;}\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(Y)_{0}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathcal{F}_{Y}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\colon\mathrm{Spin}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)_{0}.

Lemma 4.20
.

Suppose that
|H_{1}(Y;\mathbb{Z})|
is odd.
Then the map

\mathcal{F}_{Y}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\colon\mathrm{Spin}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)\xrightarrow{\;\cong\;}\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)_{0}

is a bijection. In particular, we may identify

\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant Spin structures on
Y
with
self-conjugate
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
Y
.
Moreover, there are exactly two such self-conjugate

\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
Y
,
and they differ by
1
-twisting.

Proof.

The manifold
Y
has a unique Spin structure, which we denote by
\mathfrak{s}
, and thus also a unique self-conjugate
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\mathcal{F}_{Y}(\mathfrak{s})
.
As in the case of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures, elements of
\mathrm{Spin}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)
are classified by
H^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
.
By mimicking the proof of
4.14
, we see that

H^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y;\mathbb{Z}_{2})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2},

and hence
|\mathrm{Spin}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)|=2
.
Choose one of its elements and denote it by
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
.
Then
\mathcal{F}_{Y}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
is an element of
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)_{0}
.
In particular,
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)_{0}
is nonempty.

Denote by
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y,\mathcal{F}_{Y}(\mathfrak{s}))
the set of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
Y
whose nonequivariant truncation is
\mathcal{F}_{Y}(\mathfrak{s})
.
It follows from
4.16
that
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y,\mathcal{F}_{Y}(\mathfrak{s}))
has exactly two elements, related to each other by
1
-twisting.
Since
1
-twisting clearly preserves self-conjugateness and
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)_{0}
is nonempty, we conclude that

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)_{0}=\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y,\mathcal{F}_{Y}(\mathfrak{s})).

In particular,
|\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)_{0}|=2
, and its two elements are related by
1
-twisting.

Now observe that
1
-twisting makes sense even for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant Spin structures.
Hence
\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{F}_{Y}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})
is invariant under
1
-twisting.
Since it is nonempty, we must have

\mathrm{Im}(\mathcal{F}_{Y}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}})=\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)_{0},

i.e.,
\mathcal{F}_{Y}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
is surjective.
Because

|\mathrm{Spin}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)|=|\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)_{0}|=2,

it follows that
\mathcal{F}_{Y}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
is bijective, proving the first part of the lemma.
The second part of the lemma is then immediate from the above arguments.
∎

Corollary 4.21
.

Suppose that
|H_{1}(Y;\mathbb{Z})|
is odd.
Using
4.20
, write

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y)_{0}=\{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}\}
.
Then

SWF_{\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
and

SWF_{\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})
are

(\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})
-equivariantly homotopy equivalent,
after reparametrizing the

\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action on

SWF_{\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(Y,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
by the automorphism

\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2};\qquad(x,[n])\longmapsto\left((-1)^{n}x,[n]\right).

Proof.

This follows immediately from

4.20

and the proof of

4.17
. Note that, while
4.17
is about the
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant setting, it is easy to see that the same argument also works in the
\mathrm{Pin}(2)\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant setting.
∎

4.3.
Equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
computation sequences and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-labelled planar graded roots

Let
\Gamma
be a star-shaped negative definite almost rational plumbing graph such that
Y=\partial W_{\Gamma}
is a rational homology sphere.
As reviewed in
Section

4.1
,
W_{\Gamma}
admits a smooth
S^{1}
-action that acts linearly on each disk bundle
D_{v}
(
v\in V(\Gamma)
), and in particular, acts on the disk bundle
D_{v_{c}}
corresponding to the central node
v_{c}
via fiberwise rotation.
The induced
S^{1}
-action on
Y
is the Seifert action, and, as always, we will only consider its
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-subaction.
Throughout the paper, we will also assume that
p
does not divide
|H_{1}(Y;\mathbb{Z})|=|H^{2}(Y;\mathbb{Z})|
.

Choose a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
on
Y
, and let
\mathfrak{s}=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
be its underlying
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure.
As discussed in
2.1
, by taking
v_{c}
as the base node we obtain the
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
computation sequence

\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}\bigl(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(0)\bigr),\,\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}\bigl(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{0,0}\bigr),\dots,\,\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}\bigl(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{0,n_{0}-1}\bigr),\,\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}\bigl(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(1)\bigr),\dots

of
(\Gamma,\mathfrak{s})
.
This sequence has the following properties:

•

\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,0})=\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(i))+PD[S_{v_{c}}]
;

•

\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j+1})=\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j})+PD[S_{v}]\quad\text{for some }v\in V(\Gamma)\smallsetminus\{v_{c}\}
;

•

Each
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure in the sequence restricts to
\mathfrak{s}
on
Y
.

By
4.14
, each
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure in this sequence admits a unique
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant lift restricting to
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
on
Y
.
We denote the resulting sequence of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
W_{\Gamma}
by

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\bigl(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(0)\bigr),\,\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\bigl(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{0,0}\bigr),\dots,\,\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\bigl(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{0,n_{0}-1}\bigr),\,\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\bigl(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(1)\bigr),\dots

Each
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure in this sequence now restricts to
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
on
Y
.
Moreover, by
4.12
and
4.13
, we have:

•

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,0})
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(i))
differ only in the interior of the central disk bundle
D_{v_{c}}
;

•

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j+1})
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j})
differ only in the disk bundle
D_{v}
for some
v\in V(\Gamma)\smallsetminus\{v_{c}\}
.

Remark 4.22
.

For careful readers, we provide a detailed explanation of why this construction works.
Rewrite the nonequivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
computation sequence as

\mathfrak{s}_{1},\mathfrak{s}_{2},\dots
.
By
4.14
, let

\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{2},\dots
denote their unique
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant lifts
that restrict to
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
on
Y
.
Suppose that
\mathfrak{s}_{k}
and
\mathfrak{s}_{k+1}
differ only on
D_{v}

for some node
v\in V(\Gamma)
.
Then, by
4.12
and
4.13
,
there exists a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{k+1}
on
W_{\Gamma}
,
which agrees with
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k}
outside the interior of
D_{v}
and satisfies

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{k+1})=\mathfrak{s}_{k+1}
.
Hence we have

\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k+1}|_{Y}=\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{k+1}|_{Y}=\tilde{\mathfrak{s}},\qquad\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k+1})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{k+1})=\mathfrak{s}_{k+1}.

By uniqueness (
4.14
),
it follows that
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k+1}=\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}_{k+1}
.
Therefore,
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{k+1}
differ only in the interior of
D_{v}
.

Lemma 4.23
.

For each integer
i\geq 0
, we have

n_{\mathrm{eqv}}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\!\left(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,0}\right)\right)=n_{\mathrm{eqv}}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\!\left(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(i)\right)\right)+1.

Proof.

Recall from the proof of
4.12
that we have identifications

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(D_{v_{c}})\cong\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p},\qquad\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\partial D_{v_{c}})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{w(v_{c})p},

such that the boundary restriction map

\mathrm{res}^{\partial}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is given by

(i,[j])\longmapsto[\,i+w(v_{c})j\,].

Since

\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}\!\left(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,0}\right)=\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}\!\left(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(i)\right)+PD[S_{v_{c}}]

and
w(v_{c})<0
, we see that

\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}\!\left(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,0}\right)\!\big|_{D_{v_{c}}}=\mathrm{sp}_{\mathfrak{s}}\!\left(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(i)\right)\!\big|_{D_{v_{c}}}-w(v_{c}),

as elements of
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(D_{v_{c}})\cong\mathbb{Z}
, the first summand of

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(D_{v_{c}})\cong\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Therefore, in order for the equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\!\left(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,0}\right)
and

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\!\left(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(i)\right)

to agree on
\partial D_{v_{c}}
(since they differ only in the interior of
D_{v_{c}}
),
we must have

n_{\mathrm{eqv}}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\!\left(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,0}\right)\right)=n_{\mathrm{eqv}}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\!\left(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(i)\right)\right)+1,

as claimed.
∎

Lemma 4.24
.

For any integers
i,j
with
i\geq 0
and
0\leq j<n_{i}-1
, we have

n_{\mathrm{eqv}}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\!\left(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j}\right)\right)=n_{\mathrm{eqv}}\!\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\!\left(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j+1}\right)\right).

Proof.

Choose any point
p\in S_{v_{c}}
that is not contained in
D_{v}
for any

v\in V(\Gamma)\smallsetminus\{v_{c}\}
.
Since
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
acts on
D_{v_{c}}
by fiberwise rotation,
p
is a fixed point.
Hence we may take an open ball neighborhood
U_{p}\subset D_{v_{c}}
satisfying:

•

U_{p}\cap D_{v}=\emptyset
for all
v\in V(\Gamma)\smallsetminus\{v_{c}\}
;

•

U_{p}
is setwise
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-invariant.

Now, since there exists some
v\in V(\Gamma)\smallsetminus\{v_{c}\}
such that

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\!\left(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j}\right)
and

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\!\left(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{i,j+1}\right)

differ only in the interior of
D_{v}
, the claim follows from

4.10
.
∎

We now explain how to turn this data into an enhanced version of planar graded roots, which we call
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-labelled planar graded roots
.
Given a group
G
, we say that an element

x=\sum_{g\in G}x_{g}\cdot g\in\mathbb{Z}[G]

has nonnegative coefficients
if
x_{g}\geq 0
for all
g\in G
, and we denote

|x|=\sum_{g\in G}x_{g}.

Definition 4.25
.

A
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-labelled planar graded root is a tuple

\mathcal{R}=(R,\lambda_{V},\{\lambda_{A,w}\}_{w\in V}),

with the weight function of
V(R)
denoted by
\chi
, where the following conditions are satisfied.
10
10

10

We will sometimes drop
w
from the notation if it is clear from context.

•

R
is a planar graded root with node set
V
;

•

\lambda_{V}
is a function from the set of leaves of
\mathcal{R}
to
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
;

•

For each
w\in V(R)
,
\lambda_{A,w}
is a function from
\mathrm{Angle}(w)
to
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
;

•

For each simple angle
(v,v^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Angle}(w)
, the elements

\lambda_{A,w}(v,v^{\prime})\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\lambda_{A,w}(v,v^{\prime})+\lambda_{V}(v)-\lambda_{V}(v^{\prime})

in
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
have nonnegative coefficients;

•

For each simple angle
(v,v^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Angle}(w)
, we have

|\lambda_{A,w}(v,v^{\prime})|=\chi(w)-\chi(v),\ \qquad|\lambda_{V}(v)-\lambda_{V}(v^{\prime})|=\chi(v)-\chi(v^{\prime}).

We call
R
the
underlying planar graded root
of
\mathcal{R}
,

\lambda_{V}
the
leaf labelling
of
\mathcal{R}
, and

\lambda_{A,w}
the
angle labelling
of
\mathcal{R}
at
w
.
Two
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-labelled planar graded roots are said to be
equivalent

if their underlying planar graded roots are equivalent and the leaf and angle labels agree up to an overall cyclic shift of
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
(via multiplication by a fixed element of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
) and an overall addition of leaf labels by a fixed element of
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
.

Recall from
2.6
that eventually increasing sequences of integers give rise to planar graded roots.
In a completely analogous way, we can upgrade this procedure to construct
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-labelled planar graded roots for any prime
p
.
This construction is modelled on
4.23
and
4.24
.

Definition 4.26
.

Given an eventually increasing sequence
\mathbf{n}=(n_{i})_{i\geq 0}
of integers, let
R_{\mathbf{n}}
denote the associated planar graded root.
For clarity, we write the element
[i]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
as
\tau_{p}^{i}
when describing labels.
Using the notations of
2.6
, we endow the leaves and simple angles of
R_{\mathbf{n}}
with
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-labels as follows:

•

For each
i\in I_{0}
, set

\lambda_{V}(i)=\sum_{s=0}^{i-1}(n_{s+1}-n_{s})\tau_{p}^{s}.

•

For each simple angle
\alpha_{k}=(i_{k},i_{k+1})
of
R_{\mathbf{n}}
, set

\lambda_{A}(\alpha_{k})=\sum_{s=i_{k}}^{j_{k}-1}(n_{s+1}-n_{s})\tau_{p}^{s}.

These leaf and angle labellings make
R_{\mathbf{n}}
into a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-labelled planar graded root, denoted
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{n}}
.

Since the sequence

\mathbf{x}(\Gamma,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})=\bigl\{\,\chi_{\mathfrak{s}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(0)),\,\chi_{\mathfrak{s}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(1)),\,\chi_{\mathfrak{s}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(2)),\dots\bigr\}

is eventually increasing, and

n_{\mathrm{eqv}}\bigl(\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(i))\bigr)=n_{\mathrm{eqv}}\bigl(\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(0))\bigr)+i,

it is natural to make the following definition.

Definition 4.27
.

We define the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-labelled planar graded root

\mathcal{R}_{\Gamma,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{x}(\Gamma,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}

associated to the eventually increasing integer sequence
\mathbf{x}(\Gamma,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})

as the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-labelled planar graded root of
(Y,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
.
As in the non-equivariant (i.e., unlabelled) case,
the equivalence class of
\mathcal{R}_{\Gamma,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}
depends only on
the boundary
Y
of
W_{\Gamma}
and the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
on
Y
.
However, it does depend on the chosen smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
W_{\Gamma}
.

Remark 4.28
.

In fact, the equivalence class of
\mathcal{R}_{\Gamma,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}
depends only on the non-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
on
Y
, since its definition uses only
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
. This is expected, because replacing
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
with its
k
-twist
\mathrm{tw}_{k}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
has the effect of adding
k
to the equivariance numbers of the restrictions to
D_{v_{c}}
of all equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
W_{\Gamma}
appearing in the equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
computation sequence.

4.4.
Equivariant Dirac indices, adjunction relations, and
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice model

From now on, we further assume that the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
Y
is free;
if
Y=\Sigma(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})
, this is equivalent to requiring that
p
does not divide any of
a_{1},\dots,a_{n}
.
Consider the computation sequence

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(0)),\,\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{0,0}),\,\dots,\,\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{0,n_{0}-1}),\,\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(1)),\,\dots

constructed in the previous subsection.
To convert this sequence into a lattice homotopy type,
for each
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
appearing in the sequence,
we consider the
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariant

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})\colon\bigl(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\bigr)^{+}\longrightarrow SWF(Y,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}),

as defined in
3.22
,
where
{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}
denotes the equivariant Dirac operator on
W_{\Gamma}
associated to
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
,
and
\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
denotes the topological part of its
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant index, which lies in
R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
We then glue these invariants together via adjunction relations,
following the arguments of
[
dai2023lattice
]
.

We will use the following two lemmas.

Lemma 4.29
.

Let
\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
.
Suppose there exists a based
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant (stable) map

f\colon(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{m}})^{+}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}})^{+}

such that the induced map on
S^{1}
-fixed points,

f^{S^{1}}\colon(\mathbb{C}^{0})^{+}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{0})^{+},

is a homotopy equivalence.
Then
\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{m}
has nonnegative coefficients.

Proof.

Write

\mathbf{m}=\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}m_{k}\cdot[k],\qquad\mathbf{n}=\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}n_{k}\cdot[k].

Taking the
G_{k}
-fixed point locus of
f
, we obtain an
S^{1}
-equivariant map

f^{G_{k}}\colon(\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}})^{+}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{n_{k}})^{+},

which fits into the commutative diagram

\textstyle{(\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{G_{k}}}
\textstyle{(\mathbb{C}^{n_{k}})^{+}}
\textstyle{(\mathbb{C}^{0})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{S^{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\subset}
\textstyle{(\mathbb{C}^{0})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\subset}

where the vertical arrows are the natural inclusions.

Now apply the functor
\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{S^{1}}(-;\mathbb{Q})
to this diagram.
Since there are canonical identifications

\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{S^{1}}\!\bigl((\mathbb{C}^{n})^{+};\mathbb{Q}\bigr)\cong\mathbb{Q}[U]\qquad\text{for all }n\in\mathbb{Z},

and the
S^{1}
-equivariant inclusion
(\mathbb{C}^{n})^{+}\hookrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{n+n^{\prime}})^{+}
induces multiplication by
U^{n^{\prime}}
, we obtain the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}[U]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f^{S^{1}})^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}[U]}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}[U]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f^{G_{k}})^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\times U^{n_{k}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}[U]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\times U^{m_{k}}}

Since
f^{S^{1}}
is a homotopy equivalence,
(f^{S^{1}})^{\ast}
is multiplication by some nonzero
r\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
.
Therefore,

U^{m_{k}}\,(f^{G_{k}})^{\ast}(1)=(f^{S^{1}})^{\ast}(U^{m_{k}})=r\,U^{n_{k}}.

This implies that
U^{m_{k}}
divides
U^{n_{k}}
, hence
m_{k}\leq n_{k}
.
Since this holds for all
k
, the claim follows.
∎

Lemma 4.30
.

Let
S^{0}
denote the one-point compactification of the trivial

0
-dimensional
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representation.
For some
\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
, suppose we are given
based
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant (stable) maps

f,g\colon(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{m}})^{+}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}})^{+}.

Assume further that the fixed-point maps

f^{S^{1}}\colon S^{0}\to S^{0}
and
g^{S^{1}}\colon S^{0}\to S^{0}
are homotopy equivalences, and that they are homotopic.
Then
f
and
g
are
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariantly homotopic.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\deg f^{S^{1}}=\deg g^{S^{1}}=1
.
By
3.30
and
[
dieck1987transformation
, Theorem 4.11, p. 126]
,
it suffices to show the following:

•

\deg f=\deg g
;

•

\deg f^{G_{k}}=\deg g^{G_{k}}
for each
k=1,\dots,p-1
.

We will only show that
\deg f=\deg g
, since the argument for

\deg f^{G_{k}}=\deg g^{G_{k}}
is analogous.

Forgetting the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-part of the action, we obtain
S^{1}
-equivariant stable maps

f,g\colon(\mathbb{C}^{|\mathbf{m}|})^{+}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{|\mathbf{n}|})^{+}.

By the proof of
4.29
, this forces
|\mathbf{m}|\leq|\mathbf{n}|
.
If
|\mathbf{m}|<|\mathbf{n}|
, then every map

(\mathbb{C}^{|\mathbf{m}|})^{+}\to(\mathbb{C}^{|\mathbf{n}|})^{+}
is non-equivariantly null-homotopic,
and hence
\deg f=\deg g=0
.
Thus we may assume
|\mathbf{m}|=|\mathbf{n}|
.
Choose identifications

\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{S^{1}}\bigl((\mathbb{C}^{|\mathbf{m}|})^{+};\mathbb{Z}\bigr)\cong\mathbb{Z}[U],\qquad\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{S^{1}}\bigl((\mathbb{C}^{0})^{+};\mathbb{Z}\bigr)\cong\mathbb{Z}[U].

Consider the commutative square in which the vertical maps are inclusions:

\textstyle{(\mathbb{C}^{|\mathbf{m}|})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{(\mathbb{C}^{|\mathbf{n}|})^{+}}
\textstyle{(\mathbb{C}^{0})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{S^{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\subset}
\textstyle{(\mathbb{C}^{0})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\subset}

Applying the functor
\tilde{H}^{\ast}_{S^{1}}(-;\mathbb{Z})
yields

\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}[U]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f^{S^{1}})^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}[U]}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}[U]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{U^{|\mathbf{n}|}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}[U]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\times U^{|\mathbf{m}|}}

Since
\deg f^{S^{1}}=1
, we may take
(f^{S^{1}})^{\ast}=\mathrm{id}
.
Thus

U^{|\mathbf{m}|}\cdot f^{\ast}(1)=(f^{S^{1}})^{\ast}(U^{|\mathbf{n}|})=U^{|\mathbf{n}|}=U^{|\mathbf{m}|},

which implies
f^{\ast}(1)=1
, i.e.
\deg f=1
.
The same argument applies to
g
, showing
\deg g=1
.
Therefore
\deg f=\deg g
, as required.
∎

Using
4.30
and
4.29
, we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 4.31
.

Let
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}
be
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures on
W_{\Gamma}
that agree outside the interior of
D_{v}
for some
v\in V(\Gamma)
.
Suppose that

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})+PD[S_{v}],

and that the nonequivariant topological indices of the
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
Dirac operators for

(W_{\Gamma},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))
and
(W_{\Gamma},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}))
coincide, i.e.,

\mathrm{ind}^{t}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}\simeq\mathrm{ind}^{t}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})}.

Then the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant indices of

{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}
and
{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}

are stably isomorphic as
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representations, and we have

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})\sim BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}).

Proof.

By restricting to the disk bundle
D_{v}
, we may assume without loss of generality that
\Gamma
has only one node
v
, so
W_{\Gamma}=D_{v}
. In this restricted setting, since
\Gamma
is negative definite, the weight
w(v)
of
v
is negative, which implies that
W_{\Gamma}=D_{v}
is a negative definite cobordism from
\emptyset
to
\partial D_{v}=-L(n,1)
, where
n=-w(v)
. We write

\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{D_{v},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}=\mathbf{m}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{D_{v},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}=\mathbf{m}^{\prime}

for some
\mathbf{m},\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Q}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
.

By
3.35
,
L(n,1)
admits a complete
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant metric of positive scalar curvature. Thus, by
3.14
,

SWF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(-L(n,1))\simeq(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{r}})^{+}

for some
\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{Q}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
. Therefore the equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariant for
(D_{v},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
has the form

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(D_{v},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})\colon(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{D_{v},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}})^{+}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{r}})^{+}.

Forgetting the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action gives the
S^{1}
-equivariant Bauer–Furuta invariant

BF_{S^{1}}(D_{v},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})\colon(\mathrm{ind}^{t}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{D_{v},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})})^{+}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{|\mathbf{r}|})^{+}.

Since
D_{v}
is negative definite,
(BF_{S^{1}}(D_{v},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})))^{S^{1}}
and
(BF_{S^{1}}(D_{v},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})))^{S^{1}}
are homotopy equivalences. Hence, by
4.29
,
\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{r}
and
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{r}
.

Since
H^{2}(D_{v};\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}
, write
c_{1}(\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))=k
. Because
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})+PD[S_{v}]
, we have
c_{1}(\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}))=k+2n
. Thus

\displaystyle|\mathbf{m}|

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}\bigl(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{D_{v},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}\bigr)=-\frac{k^{2}-n}{8n},

\displaystyle|\mathbf{m}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}\bigl(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{D_{v},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})}\bigr)=-\frac{(k+2n)^{2}-n}{8n}.

Hence
k^{2}=(k+2n)^{2}
, i.e.,
4n(n+k)=0
. Since
n\neq 0
, we deduce
k=-n
, which implies

|\mathbf{m}|=-\frac{n^{2}-n}{8n}=|\mathbf{m}^{\prime}|.

On the other hand, by the
d
-invariant formula for lens spaces
[
ozsvath2003absolutely
, Proposition 4.8]
11
11

11

Here we actually need to calculate the monopole Frøyshov invariant
\delta
of lens spaces. However, as noted in
[
LRS18
, Remark 1.1]
, the isomorphisms between monopole and Heegaard Floer homologies preserve
\mathbb{Q}
-gradings. Thus, for any
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
rational homology 3-sphere
(Y,\mathfrak{s})
,

\delta(Y,\mathfrak{s})=\tfrac{1}{2}d(Y,\mathfrak{s}).

we have

|\mathbf{r}|=\frac{1}{2}d(-L(n,1),\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})|_{\partial D_{v}})=-\frac{n^{2}-n}{8n},

so
|\mathbf{m}|=|\mathbf{m}^{\prime}|=|\mathbf{r}|
. Since
\mathbf{m}\leq\mathbf{r}
and
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{r}
, we conclude
\mathbf{m}=\mathbf{m}^{\prime}=\mathbf{r}
. Thus

\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{D_{v},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\simeq\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{D_{v},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}},

proving the first part of the lemma.

Finally, observe that
\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})=\overline{\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}
, so

\deg(BF_{S^{1}}(D_{v},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})))^{S^{1}}=\deg(BF_{S^{1}}(D_{v},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})))^{S^{1}}.

Hence
BF_{S^{1}}(D_{v},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}))\sim BF_{S^{1}}(D_{v},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}))
. Since
D_{v}
is negative definite, these are homotopy equivalences. The second part of the lemma therefore follows from
4.30
.
∎

Next, we consider the disk bundle
X:=D_{v_{c}}
for the central node
v_{c}
of
\Gamma
.
Let
Y
be the boundary of
X
. In this case, as discussed in
Section

4.1
, the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-action on
D_{v_{c}}
is given by the fiberwise rotation. Recall from
4.7
that we have an identification

\mathrm{Spin}^{c}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X)\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\cong\;\;}\;\mathrm{Spin}^{c}(X)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p};\qquad\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}\longmapsto\left(\,\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}),\,n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})\,\right).

Thus, for any
n\in w(v_{c})+2\mathbb{Z}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we will denote by
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{n,\alpha}
the unique
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
D_{v_{c}}
such that
c_{1}(\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{n,\alpha}))=n
(where we identify
H^{2}(D_{v_{c}};\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}
) and
n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{n,\alpha})=\alpha
.

Consider the generator
\gamma=[1]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. We compute the Lefschetz number difference

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{k,s}&=\mathrm{Tr}_{\gamma}\!\left(\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{-n+2k,[s]}}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}_{\gamma}\!\left(\mathrm{ind}^{\mathrm{APS}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{n+2k,[s-1]}}\right)\\
&=\mathrm{Tr}_{\gamma}\!\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{-n+2k,[s]}}\right)-\mathrm{Tr}_{\gamma}\!\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{n+2k,[s-1]}}\right),\end{split}

for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Here the second equality holds because, by
4.11
, the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structures
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{-n+2k,[s]}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{n+2k,[s-1]}
on
D_{v_{c}}=\nu(S_{v_{c}})
restrict to the same
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
\partial D_{v_{c}}
.
Note also that the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-fixed point set of
D_{v_{c}}
does not meet
\partial D_{v_{c}}
.
Therefore, by
, the
\gamma
-trace of the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant index can be computed as

\begin{split}\zeta_{2p}^{-1}\mathrm{ind}_{\gamma}^{t}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{-n+2k,[s]}})&=-\tfrac{1}{4}\,\zeta_{p}^{s}\!\left(i(-n+2k)\csc\tfrac{\pi}{p}+n\csc\tfrac{\pi}{p}\cot\tfrac{\pi}{p}\right)\\
&=-\tfrac{1}{4}\,\zeta_{p}^{s}\csc^{2}\tfrac{\pi}{p}\!\left(n\cos\tfrac{\pi}{p}+i(-n+2k)\sin\tfrac{\pi}{p}\right),\\
\zeta_{2p}^{-1}\mathrm{ind}_{\gamma}^{t}({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{n+2k,[s-1]}})&=-\tfrac{1}{4}\,\zeta_{p}^{\,s-1}\csc^{2}\tfrac{\pi}{p}\!\left(n\cos\tfrac{\pi}{p}+i(n+2k)\sin\tfrac{\pi}{p}\right).\end{split}

Hence we obtain

\begin{split}\zeta_{2p}^{-1}\mathcal{I}_{k,s}&=\mathrm{ind}_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{APS}}\!\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{-n+2k,[s]}}\right)-\mathrm{ind}_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{APS}}\!\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{n+2k,[s-1]}}\right)\\
&=\tfrac{\zeta_{p}^{s-1}\csc^{2}\!\tfrac{\pi}{p}}{4}\left(-\zeta_{p}\!\left(n\cos\tfrac{\pi}{p}+i(-n+2k)\sin\tfrac{\pi}{p}\right)+\left(n\cos\tfrac{\pi}{p}+i(n+2k)\sin\tfrac{\pi}{p}\right)\right)\\
&=\tfrac{\zeta_{p}^{s-1}\csc^{2}\!\tfrac{\pi}{p}}{4}\left(-\zeta_{p}\!\left(n\zeta_{2p}^{-1}+2ik\sin\tfrac{\pi}{p}\right)+\left(n\zeta_{2p}+2ik\sin\tfrac{\pi}{p}\right)\right)\\
&=\tfrac{ik\,\zeta_{p}^{s-1}\csc\tfrac{\pi}{p}}{2}(1-\zeta_{p}).\end{split}

Since

\zeta_{p}-1=\bigl(\cos\tfrac{2\pi}{p}-1\bigr)+i\sin\tfrac{2\pi}{p}=2\sin\tfrac{\pi}{p}\bigl(-\sin\tfrac{\pi}{p}+i\cos\tfrac{\pi}{p}\bigr)=2i\zeta_{2p}\sin\tfrac{\pi}{p},

we deduce

\mathcal{I}_{k,s}=\tfrac{ik\,\zeta_{p}^{s-1}\csc\tfrac{\pi}{p}}{2}(1-\zeta_{p})=\zeta_{2p}\cdot\tfrac{ik\,\zeta_{p}^{s-1}\csc\tfrac{\pi}{p}}{2}\cdot\bigl(-2i\zeta_{2p}\sin\tfrac{\pi}{p}\bigr)=k\zeta_{p}^{s-1}\zeta_{2p}^{2}=k\zeta_{p}^{s}.

Using this computation, we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 4.32
.

For any
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we have

\mathbb{C}_{[s]}^{-k}\oplus\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\!\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{-n+2k,[s]}}\right)\simeq\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\!\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{n+2k,[s-1]}}\right).

Proof.

We begin with the difference of complex dimensions:

\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}\!\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{-n+2k,[s]})}\right)-\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}\!\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{n+2k,[s]})}\right)=\frac{-(-n+2k)^{2}+n}{8n}-\frac{-(n+2k)^{2}+n}{8n}=k.

Hence

\mathbb{C}^{-k}\oplus\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\!\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}_{-n+2k,[s]}}\right)\simeq\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\!\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}_{n+2k,[s-1]}}\right).

Thus there exists some
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{Q}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
with
|\mathbf{n}|=-k
such that

\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{n}}\,\oplus\,\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\!\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{-n+2k,[s]}}\right)\simeq\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\!\left({\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{\nu(S_{v_{c}}),\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}_{n+2k,[s-1]}}\right).

Writing
\mathbf{n}=\sum_{t=0}^{p-1}n_{t}\cdot[t]
, the trace relation from the computation of
\mathcal{I}_{k,s}
gives

\sum_{t=0}^{p-1}n_{t}\zeta_{p}^{t}=-\mathcal{I}_{k,s}=-k\zeta_{p}^{s}.

Case
p=2
:

The relations reduce to

n_{[0]}+n_{[1]}=-k,\qquad n_{[0]}-n_{[1]}=-(-1)^{s}k.

Solving, we find

\mathbf{n}=-k\left(\tfrac{1+(-1)^{s}}{2}\cdot[0]+\tfrac{1-(-1)^{s}}{2}\cdot[1]\right)=-k\cdot[s],

as desired.

Case
p>2
:

The relation implies that
\zeta_{p}
is a root of the polynomial

kx^{s}+(n_{0}+n_{1}x+\cdots+n_{p-1}x^{p-1})\in\mathbb{Q}[x].

Since the minimal polynomial of
\zeta_{p}
over
\mathbb{Q}
is
1+x+\cdots+x^{p-1}
, there exists some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

m(1+x+\cdots+x^{p-1})=kx^{s}+(n_{0}+n_{1}x+\cdots+n_{p-1}x^{p-1}).

Evaluating at
x=1
, we obtain

pm=k+(n_{0}+n_{1}+\cdots+n_{p-1})=k+|\mathbf{n}|=0,

so
m=0
. Thus

n_{0}+n_{1}x+\cdots+n_{p-1}x^{p-1}=-kx^{s},

which means
\mathbf{n}=-k\cdot[s]
, completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.33
.

Let
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure on
W_{\Gamma}
, and let

\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}
be another such structure which agrees with
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
outside the interior of
D_{v_{c}}
and satisfies

\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})=\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})+PD[S_{v_{c}}].

Consider the index difference

\Delta_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}=\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}\!\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})}\right)-\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}\!\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\mathcal{N}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}\right).

Then there is a stable equivalence

\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime}}\simeq\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{\Delta_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}}_{\,n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}.

Furthermore, if we denote by
U_{\alpha}
the stable
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant homotopy class of the inclusion

(\mathbb{C}^{0})^{+}\hookrightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{1\cdot\alpha})^{+}

for each
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, then we have
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant homotopies

\displaystyle BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})\circ U^{\Delta_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}}_{\,n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}

\displaystyle\sim BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})

\displaystyle\text{if }\Delta_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}>0,

\displaystyle BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\sim BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})

\displaystyle\text{if }\Delta_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}=0,

\displaystyle BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\sim BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})\circ U^{-\Delta_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}}_{\,n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}

\displaystyle\text{if }\Delta_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}<0.

Proof.

As in the proof of
4.31
,
we may assume that
\Gamma
has only one node, so that
W_{\Gamma}=D_{v_{c}}
.
Then the first part of the lemma follows directly from

4.32
.

For the second part, we may assume without loss of generality that
\Delta_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\geq 0
.
Define

f=BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(D_{v_{c}},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\prime})\circ U^{\Delta_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}}_{\,n_{\mathrm{eqv}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad g=BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(D_{v_{c}},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}).

Then, by the first part of the lemma,
we may regard
f
and
g
as
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant stable maps of the form

f,g\colon\mathrm{ind}^{t}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{D_{v_{c}},\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}\longrightarrow\left(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{r}}\right)^{+}

for some
\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{Q}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]
.
Since
D_{v_{c}}
is negative definite, we know that

f^{S^{1}}
and
g^{S^{1}}
are homotopy equivalences that are homotopic.
Therefore
f
and
g
are
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariantly homotopic
by
4.30
.
∎

Recall that, in
Section

4.3
, given a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
structure
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}
on
Y=\partial W_{\Gamma}
, we have constructed the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant
\mathrm{Spin}^{c}
computation sequence

\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(0)),\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{0,0}),\dots,\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x^{\mathfrak{s}}_{0,n_{0}-1}),\ \widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(1)),\dots

For simplicity, we assume that

n_{\mathrm{eqv}}\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(0))\right)=[0]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p};

otherwise, we simply cyclically permute the elements of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
during our construction. We will also rewrite the above sequence as follows:

\mathfrak{s}_{1},\mathfrak{s}_{2},\mathfrak{s}_{3},\dots

We already know that the integer sequence
\left(\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{s}_{i})}\right)\right)_{i\geq 0}
is eventually increasing, so we may choose an integer
N>0
such that the sequence is increasing after its
N
th term. For each integer
i\geq 0
, we define the non-equivariant index difference sequence:

\Delta_{i}=\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\mathfrak{s}_{i+1}}\right)-\alpha_{\mathbb{C}}\left(\mathrm{ind}^{t}{\mathcal{D}\mkern-11.0mu/}_{W_{\Gamma},\mathfrak{s}_{i}}\right).

Then, by
3.31
,
4.23
,
4.24
,
4.31
and
4.33
, we see that the sequence
(\mathfrak{s}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
has the following properties.

•

For each
i\geq 0
, there exists a node
v_{i}\in V(\Gamma)
such that

\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{s}_{i+1})=\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{s}_{i})+PD[S_{v_{i}}].

•

If
\Delta_{i}=0
, then by
3.35
,
3.14
and
4.29
, there exists a stable
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant homotopy

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\mathfrak{s}_{i+1})\sim BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\mathfrak{s}_{i}).

•

If
\Delta_{i}>0
, then
\mathfrak{s}_{i+1}=\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(k_{i}))
for some
k_{i}\geq 0
, and there exists a stable
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant homotopy

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\mathfrak{s}_{i+1})\circ U^{\Delta_{i}}_{[k_{i}]}\sim BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\mathfrak{s}_{i}).

•

If
\Delta_{i}<0
, then
\mathfrak{s}_{i}=\widetilde{\mathrm{sp}}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}(x_{\mathfrak{s}}(k_{i}))
for some
k_{i}\geq 0
, and there exists a stable
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant homotopy

BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\mathfrak{s}_{i+1})\sim BF_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(W_{\Gamma},\mathfrak{s}_{i})\circ U^{-\Delta_{i}}_{[k_{i}]}.

Using these properties, we can now describe an
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant lattice homotopy type model for
Y
as follows. For each
i
, we define a sequence of virtual
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representations
V_{i}
and
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant virtual linear maps

f_{i}\colon\begin{cases}V_{i}\longrightarrow V_{i+1}\quad&\text{if }\Delta_{i}\geq 0,\\
V_{i+1}\longrightarrow V_{i}\quad&\text{if }\Delta_{i}<0,\end{cases}

as follows. We start by defining
V_{0}=0
, the zero representation. Suppose that we have defined
V_{0},\dots,V_{i}
. Then we define
V_{i+1}
in the following manner:

\displaystyle V_{i+1}

\displaystyle=V_{i}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{\Delta_{i}}_{[k_{i}]},

\displaystyle\qquad f_{i}

\displaystyle=U^{\Delta_{i}}_{[k_{i}]},

\displaystyle\qquad\text{if }\Delta_{i}>0,

\displaystyle V_{i+1}

\displaystyle=V_{i},

\displaystyle\qquad f_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{id},

\displaystyle\qquad\text{if }\Delta_{i}=0,

\displaystyle V_{i}

\displaystyle=V_{i+1}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{-\Delta_{i}}_{[k_{i}]},

\displaystyle\qquad f_{i}

\displaystyle=U^{-\Delta_{i}}_{[k_{i}]},

\displaystyle\qquad\text{if }\Delta_{i}<0.

This defines
V_{0},\dots,V_{N}
and
f_{0},\dots,f_{N-1}
, which induce
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-spectra
(V_{i})^{+}
and
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant stable maps
f_{i}^{+}
between them. For simplicity, denote the domain of
f_{i}^{+}
by
W_{i}^{+}
. Using these data, we build an
S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}
–CW complex
\mathcal{H}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Gamma,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
as follows:

\mathcal{H}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Gamma,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})=\left(\bigvee_{i=0}^{N}V_{i}^{+}\right)\;\vee\;\left(\bigvee_{i=0}^{N-1}(W_{i}^{+}\wedge[0,1])\right)\big/\sim,

where we take quotients by identifying the ends of cylinders
W_{i}^{+}\wedge[0,1]
with spheres
V_{i}^{+}
and
V_{i+1}^{+}
as follows.

•

If
W_{i}^{+}=V_{i}^{+}
, identify
W_{i}^{+}\times\{0\}
with
V_{i}^{+}
via the identity map, and attach
W_{i}^{+}\times\{1\}
to
V_{i+1}^{+}
via
f_{i}^{+}
.

•

If
W_{i}^{+}=V_{i+1}^{+}
, identify
W_{i}^{+}\times\{0\}
with
V_{i+1}^{+}
via the identity map, and attach
W_{i}^{+}\times\{1\}
to
V_{i}^{+}
via
f_{i}^{+}
.

Definition 4.34
.

We denote the resulting
(S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-spectrum by
\mathcal{H}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Gamma,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
.

Remark 4.35
.

\mathcal{H}_{S^{1}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\Gamma,\tilde{\mathfrak{s}})
can also be described categorically as follows:

3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 199.50478pt\raise-8.49054pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern
